# Luke

## ANNOTATIONS ON THE GOSPEL ACCORDING TO S. LUKE

## THE ARGUMENT

The Gospel is the gladsome tidings of salvation by Christ: this Gospel (as the penman himself interprets, Acts 1. 1.) is the history of *those things that Jesus began to do and teach, until the day that he was taken up, &c.* That is as much thereof, as God saw good to record by his ministry: that (as the other Evangelist says) we might believe that *Jesus is the Christ the Son of God, and that in believing, we might have life through his Name.* John 20. 30, 31.

This sacred history was dictated by the Holy Ghost, whose penman was Luke.

Luke was of Antioch in Syria, by profession a physician, (Col. 4. 14.) he was a follower and faithful assistant of Paul (2 Timothy 4. 11.) and so became also a physician for the soul: but who was the first ministerial means of his conversion, is not certain: some think he was one of the seventy disciples: and that other unnamed, Luke 24. 18. who with Cleophas after Christ's resurrection, went to Emmaus: if so, he had had Christ the best teacher: but others think Paul converted him at Thebes: but himself best shows in his preface, what instructors he had, those who from the beginning were eyewitnesses and ministers of the same Gospel: Luke 1. 2. Whence it is evident, that some of the Apostles instructed him in the mysteries of salvation by Christ, and faithfully related to him this whole story.

Matthew it seems wrote to the Hebrews, and the other three Evangelists to the Gentiles in the Greek tongue, wherein Luke in an elegant style, dedicates two excellent pieces, (this Gospel, and the Acts of the Apostles) unto Theophilus, an honorable person, and in him to all the faithful, who are lovers of God.

The occasion of his writing, (whether he were incited thereto by Peter, as some think, or, by Paul, as others) was (that which he declares in his preface) *for as much as many have taken in hand to set forth the Story of those things, whereof we are fully perswaded—it seemed good also to me,—&c.* Luke 1. 1. Thus God by the unanimous consent of a sufficient number of witnesses (writing at divers places, and times) pleased to confirm the truth of the Gospel unto us.

Concerning the matter and subject of this Gospel, we must know, that the Evangelist purposed to declare, that Jesus the Son of the Virgin Mary, was that promised seed (whose forerunning messenger was John Baptist, according to the prophecies:) whose admirable conception by the Holy Ghost, made not against the truth of his human nature, and nativity, according to the flesh: who in the fullness of time (the scepter being departed from Judah) in the forty-second year of the reign of Augustus Caesar, was born in Bethlehem Judah, grew up to man's stature and perfection, undertook his public ministry and office of the Messiah, showing himself in doctrine and miracles, (specially, in his glorious resurrection from the dead, and ascension into heaven) truly, God and man, in unity of person, the Savior and Redeemer of the elect: all which he relates in this order.

1. In the three first chapters, he rehearses his conception, nativity, and the witnesses thereof; to which he annexes his genealogy.
2. From the fourth chapter, to the tenth, he sets down the preparation to his ministry, in his temptation, the beginning of his preaching, miracles, ordination of Apostles, transfiguration on the mount, and departure out of Galilee.
3. From the tenth chapter, to the twentieth, he records what Christ did in his last passage towards Jerusalem.
4. In the twentieth, and the following chapter, he tells what he taught in the temple, his disputes with the Pharisees and Sadducees, as also of the destruction of the city and republic of Israel, where he inserts some things concerning his second coming to judgment.
5. Thence he comes to the history of his passion, Chap. 22. 23. The conclusion and lowest degree of his obedience and humiliation.
6. The last chapter, contains the truth of his resurrection, his apparitions to his disciples, specially the two going to Emmaus, and his glorious ascension into heaven, the assured pledge of our interest therein, whereof he has in our nature taken livery and seisin.

## CHAPTER 1

**Verse 1. ***{Many have taken in hand}* It seems that Matthew and Mark wrote not before Luke, had they, he would have spoken more reverently concerning them: whether he means by *many*, those counterfeit pieces under the titles of the Gospel of Nicodemus, Thomas, according to the Egyptians, or according to the twelve; it is uncertain; because he names neither these, nor others like: but affirming a full assurance of that which he (with the other holy Evangelists) writes, he seems modestly to deny, that others had the same.

*{to set forth in order}* To compose and write the history of Christ, his life and doctrine.

*{which are most surely believed}* Of which we were assured, or so certified, that we nothing doubt of their truth.

**V. 2. ***{they delivered them unto us}* Meaning the Apostles, who continually attending on our Savior Christ, saw these miracles and heard his doctrine, which this Evangelist conversing with them, by the infallible assistance of the Holy Ghost, and their relations, here records: for he was not *ἀπ᾽* *ἀρχῆς* *μαθητής*, *ἀλλ᾽* *ὑστερόχρονος*, which Theophylact notes, not a disciple from the beginning, but of later time.

*{were eyewitnesses}* Of those things which Christ did, see 1 John 1. 1. Heb. 2. 3.

*{Ministers of the Word}* He means either of the Gospel (whereof themselves were a part) or of Christ, the eternal Word, John 1. 1. 1 John 1. 1.

**V. 3. ***{it seemed good to me}* By a divine inspiration, as Acts 15. 25, 28. See also 1 Cor. 7. 40. 2 Tim. 3. 16.

*{most excellent Theophilus}* This was some excellent and renowned person in the Church of those times: this title is given to honorable persons, and men in authority, Acts 23. 26.

**V. 4. ***{that thou mightest know the certainty}* He having exactly learned all these things now writes them to the further edification of Theophilus, something before informed hereof by word of mouth.

**V. 5. ***{Herod King of Judaea}* Herod the great, son of Antipater; not Herod Tetrarch of Galilee, mentioned, Luke 3. 1.

*{of the course of Abia}* See 1 Chron. 24. 5, 10. David appointed the sons of Aaron to minister by turns: (See on Mark 14. 1.) or several courses.

*{daughters of Aaron}* Of the posterity of Aaron, verse 36. Elizabeth is called the cousin of Mary, though she were of the tribe of Judah and David's family; for that law Numb. 36. 8. which cautioned that the daughter should marry in her own tribe, lest there should be confusion in the inheritances of the tribes; concerned them only that possessed an inheritance, in any tribe of the children of Israel, and forbade not any of the tribes to make affinity and marry with a daughter of a Levite; who had no inheritance in that land assigned them, Numb. 18. 20.

**V. 6. ***{they were both righteous before God}* That is, of sincere lives; approved of God; as Job was, Job 1. 8. not without all sin, but without blame before men. See Philippians 3. 6, 12. Such were they as God required Abraham to be, Genesis 17. 1. upright and sincere, free from hypocrisy: and justified by faith in Christ. See Rom. 4. 22.

*{walking in all}* That is, living in, and endeavoring to keep all the commandments. See Psalm 119. 1, 6. not that they attained perfection by the works of the law. (See Phil. 3. 12.) but as he that walks in a strait way, sometimes stumbles, falls, and does not presently come to the end of that way, but by some other supply of his lameness or infirmity; so is it here.

**V. 9. ***{According to the custom}* See note on verse 5. and according to God's law, this office appertained to Aaron and his sons, Exod. 30. 7. 1 Chron. 6. 49.

*{into the temple of the Lord}* Into that court which was called holy; into which only the priests entered, Exod. 28. 43.

**V. 10. ***{The whole multitude}* See Levit. 16. 17. then assembled to the worship of God; in that part of the temple, which was called *atrium Judæorum*, because thither the Jews might come to the public service of God in the temple. God could have privately, at his own house secretly, revealed these things to Zachary, concerning Elizabeth his wife's conception of the great forerunner of Christ, John Baptist: but it pleased him to reserve the same to such a time and place, as might by many witnesses of those things, which then and there fell out, render this vision of Zachary, more famous and public for the stirring up the minds of the people to attend the event thereof.

*{without}* In that part of the temple which was called the people's court. See Leviticus 16. 17. 1 Kings 7. 12.

*{the time of incense}* See Exod. 30. 1. That is, while the priest, according to the law, burned incense within: this incense was to be offered morning and evening by the priest, when he dressed the lamps, Exodus 30. 7, 8. not without a singular mystery; for the lamps signified the light of God's Word, the incense prayers, therewith to be joined, and that the prayers of God's people are a sweet spiritual incense acceptable unto him (Psalm 141. 2.) being presented by Christ our High Priest ministering in the true tabernacle, which the Lord pitched, Heb. 8. 2. appearing in his presence for us, Heb. 9. 24. offered in the golden censer of the Angel of the Covenant Christ. See Revel. 8. 3, 4. these, as that incense offered morning and evening, ought to be continual. 1 Thess. 5. 17. as our High Priest, ever liveth to make intercession for us, Hebrews 7. 25.

**V. 12. ***{And fear fell upon him}* The sudden and unexpected sight of the Angel not only troubled, but terrified him. So Dan. 10. 8. if the appearance of one Angel sent to comfort, so much affrighted this just man, in the work of his holy calling, what dread and terror shall fall upon the wicked when Christ (God and man) shall appear with all his Angels to judge the wicked, for all their willful acts of impiety?

**V. 13. ***{Thou shalt call his name John}* This is not only a prediction but a precept, and the imposition of a name before the nativity, was ever an argument of God's marking those parties to some excellence. See Matth. 1. 21. this name signifies the grace of God; intimating that which should be in his person and office. See note on John 1. 6. So that Zachary's prayer was heard in that the Messiah should now come; and in that Elizabeth, for whom he had often prayed, should also conceive and bear a son.

**V. 14. ***{And thou shalt have joy, &c.}* So great, as that it shall break out into rejoicing; for that you shall obtain a desired issue, above hope and the common course of nature, and for that you shall know the signal office, to which this child is assigned, that is, to be the immediate herald to proclaim the Messiah, and the forerunner to prepare his ways. And this joy shall not be only domestic, but also public, as concerning the interest of all those which expect Christ the Savior of Israel.

**V. 15. ***{great in the sight of the Lord}* That is, God shall highly esteem him, and make him excellent.

*{shall neither drink wine nor strong drink}* *σίκερα*, which Theophylact, Euthymius, etc. interpret *πᾶν* *τὸ* *μέθην* *ἐμποιεῖν* *δυνάμενον*, anything which may make drunken: so also Jerome lib. 2. ep. 14. Nepotiano. The word is taken from the Hebrew *שכר* *inebriatus fuit*, thence *שכר*, *shechar*, and in the Syriac *שכרא*, Levit. 10. 9. Aaron and his sons were forbid to drink *יין* wine, and also *שכר* which the 70 give *σίκερα*; as here, so where Hanna said she had drunk neither wine nor *שכר* strong drink: the 70 give it *μέθυσμα* the Syriac by *שכר* understand also, *fœdum, deformem*, a filthy or deformed person: such are they who are overcome with drink: and therefore the Lacedaemonians were wont to make their Helots, or slaves drunken; that themselves being sober, and observing *τὴν* *ἐκείνων* *ἀσχημοσύνην* their uncomely and filthy behavior, it might be a remedy to them; causing them to loathe that sin, the effects whereof were so shameful and loathsome. See Clemens Alexandrinus Pædag. lib. 3. cap. 8. The meaning of the Angel is here, that John should be consecrated unto God, as a Nazarite. See Numb. 6. 3. Judges 13. 4, 5. 1 Sam. 1. 11.

*{filled with the Holy Ghost}* That is, he shall have an abundant measure of the Spirit of sanctification even from his mother's womb; and in his ripe age, for the execution of his charge. See Jeremiah 1. 5. Galatians 1. 15. Seeing then infants are capable of grace, and the Holy Ghost, we may say for them as Peter for his hearers, Acts 10. 47. Can any man forbid water, that these should not be baptized, which have received the Holy Ghost, as well as we? See note on Acts 10. 47.

**V. 16. ***{many of the children of Israel}* See Malachi 4. 6. He shall be the means of their conversion, by his preaching, accompanied with the power of the Holy Ghost, to make it effectual. See Acts 26. 18. 1 Corinthians 3. 5, 6. James 5. 19, 20.

**V. 17. ***{he shall goe before him, &c.}* That is, immediately before Christ the Messiah, the King of Israel, to declare his coming, and presence. See John 1. 29. Malachi 4. 5. Matthew 17. 14.

*{in the spirit and power of Elias}* That is, with as great a measure of grace, and gifts of God's Spirit, to make his ministry powerful. See Matth. 11. 11, 14. Mal. 4. 6.

*{turn the hearts of the fathers to their children}* By his ministry, the unnatural, degenerate, and wicked children, shall be converted, and become obedient children to their fathers, and to the Church of Christ; so, as their fathers shall acknowledge them to be their children indeed: for the division, which follows the Gospel, that is, an accidental effect, which the corruption of man produces, and not the Gospel of peace.

*{to the wisdom}* *ἐν* *φρονήσει*, here *ἐν* is put for *εἰς*. So the interpreter of the Syriac, *ad cognitionem rectorum*, the knowledge of right things. Jerome, on Mal. 4. gives the meaning. *Ut credant posteri eorum in Dominum Salvatorem in quem & illi crediderunt*, that their posterity may believe in the Lord their Savior, in whom (Abraham, Isaac and Jacob) themselves believed: the whole sense is, by the example of their holy fathers, they shall be wise and believe, and so be of one heart and consent with their fathers.

*{prepared for the Lord}* Prepared for Christ, as John proclaimed chap. 3. 4.

**V. 18. ***{For I am an old man, &c.}* Considering the ordinary course of nature, he was slow in believing so great a miracle. Abraham laying aside all natural reason, believed above hope. See Rom. 4. 17.

**V. 9. ***{I am Gabriel}* *גבריאל* *fortitudo Dei*. This name signifies the power of God: he put him in mind, that he was the same Angel, who some hundred years before, was sent to the Prophet Daniel, to declare the time of Christ's coming, Dan. 8. 16. Dan. 9. 21. that he might assure him of the fulfilling of the same, now in the fullness of time.

*{that stand in the presence of God}* This Hebraism, borrowed from those who wait on kings, imports a readiness to administer or serve. See 1 Kings 10. 8. Prov. 22. 29.

**V. 20. ***{thou shalt be dumb}* Because you desire a sign, this shall be both a punishment of your incredulity, and a sign to confirm this faith, as Chrysologus Ser. 90. says, *Ut ex te & in te credendi regulam consequaris*: that from yourself, and in yourself, you must attain a rule of believing.

**V. 21. ***{waited for Zachary}* For he was to dismiss them with a blessing. See Numb. 6. 23. Now they waited longer than ordinary, without the holy place, to which the people of the Jews were to resort, as verse 10.

**V. 22. ***{he could not speak}* Not only because a marvelous stupor or fear had invaded him, but because God had, for the time, taken away his faculty of speaking, for a sign unto him (that he might believe) and to the people, that at the preaching of Christ now at hand, their priesthood should be silenced.

*{they perceived that he had seen a vision}* By his silence, pale countenance, beckoning with, and lifting up his hand toward heaven, or the holy place whence he came, by these or the like means God would prepare the people to conceive a high opinion of John the forerunner of Christ.

**V. 23. ***{were accomplished}* At Jerusalem he was to officiate one whole week. See 2 Kings 11. 1. Chron. 9. 25. which ended, he went home.

**V. 24. ***{hid herself}* She kept her chamber, or otherwise concealed her miraculous conception so long, until it was evident, and out of doubt that she had indeed conceived.

**V. 25. ***{to take away my reproach}* For barrenness was counted a dishonor to the women. See 1 Sam. 1. 6, 11.

**V. 26. ***{the sixth month}* Reckoning from Elizabeth's conception.

**V. 27. ***{to a virgin espoused}* That is, promised in marriage: a woman so promised was said *μνηστεύεσθαι*. See Mat. 1. 18. and here, chap. 2. 5.

*{of the house of David}* Of the stock and lineage of David, (so some Greek copies have it) as was also Joseph, Luke 2. 4. Matth. 1. 16.

**V. 28. ***{highly favoured}* Or *graciously accepted*, *much graced* (see verse 30.) *freely beloved*: as the Apostle uses the word, Ephes. 1. 6. honored with this singular grace, to be the mother of the Messiah: therefore she said she was blessed among women: he said not, be honored as God: *οὔτε* *γὰρ* *Θεὸς* *ἡ* *Μαρία*, says Epiphanius *adv. hæres.* l. 3. tom. 2. 23. for neither was Mary a God.

**V. 29. ***{she was troubled}* At the appearance of the heavenly majesty which probably shined in the Angel, and at that strange salutation.

**V. 30. ***{thou hast found favour}* You are in God's favor, who has freely chosen you to this honor which you thought not of.

**V. 31. ***{and behold thou shalt conceive}* See Isa. 7. 14.

*{his name Jesus}* See Matthew 1. 21. a Savior of his people.

**V. 32. ***{the son of the highest}* So he shall be called, and so is he from all eternity: the only Son of God by nature: God manifested in the flesh, 1 Tim. 3. 16.

*{the throne of his father David}* The spiritual and eternal kingdom over his Church, prefigured in David's temporal kingdom over Israel. See Isaiah 9. 7. Jerem. 30. 9. Ezekiel 34. 24. Ezekiel 37. 24. Hosea 3. 5. Thus the Angel declares, that this is he in whom the prophecy of Amos 9. 11. concerning the raising up of the decayed tabernacle of David, should be fulfilled.

**V. 33. ***{and he shall reign}* See Daniel 7. 14. Micha 4. 7. This spiritual kingdom of Christ is begun in this world, in, and over the saints, by the grace of his Spirit, regenerating and governing them; and shall be perpetuated in his kingdom of glory; uniting them unto himself the head of his Church, and making them thereby fully and eternally blessed.

**V. 34. ***{How shall this be?}* This question was not of diffidence, but rather of admiration: for though the Prophet declared this mystery of Christ's incarnation by a virgin, Isaiah 7. 14. and of the branch arising out of the decayed stock of Jesse, Isaiah 11. 1. yet the manner how was not fully understood, hence she desires resolution: and it seems they were not to come together presently after betrothing.

**V. 35. ***{overshadow thee}* The extraordinary power of God shall effect this: it seems an allusion to that, 1 Kings 8. 12.

*{holy thing}* Christ without all sin: as God, the eternal Son of the Father: as man, conceived of a sanctified mass, or substance without all sin.

**V. 36. ***{thy cousin Elizabeth}* See note on verse 4.

*{who was called barren}* Elizabeth who was known or noted to be barren, is now six months gone with child.

**V. 37. ***{nothing shall be impossible}* See Genes. 18. 14. Jeremiah 31. 17. Matthew 19. 26. in the Greek it is *πᾶν* *ῥῆμα*, every word; that is after the Hebrew expression, no word. So Psalm 143. 2. *כל־חי*, all living; that is, none living: and so they put *דבר* *dabar*, a word, for a work, or thing, as Genes. 24. 50. *from the Lord proceedeth* *הדבר*, 70. *τὸ* *πρᾶγμα*, the thing. In these words, the Angel lifts up the virgin's mind to the consideration of God's omnipotency, that she might rest secure concerning the fulfilling of the promise, which he brought her.

**V. 38. ***{behold the handmaid of the Lord}* This is also an Hebrew manner of speaking, importing a ready obedience and submission. See 1 Samuel 3. 4. *הנני* *hinneni*, behold I, or here am I, Isaiah 6. 8.

**V. 39. ***{to the hill country}* To Zachary and Elizabeth's house, which probably was Kirioth-Arba, or Hebron assigned to priests, see Josh. 14. 15. Josh. 21. 11.

**V. 41. ***{The babe leaped}* This extraordinary motion was by the inspiration of the Holy Ghost, enabling Elizabeth to know and prophesy the incarnation of Christ; (for no otherwise could she know it than by divine revelation) and showing a secret correspondence between Christ and John rejoicing at his coming.

**V. 42. ***{blessed art thou}* So the Angel pronounced verse 28. she was blessed, in that God not only vouchsafed her the honor of conceiving her Savior in her womb but chiefly for that he gave her faith to believe in her Son and Savior Jesus Christ. See verse 45. and Chap. 11. 27, 28.

**V. 43. ***{whence is this to me, that the mother of my Lord, &c.}* Elizabeth respecting God's great favors conferred on the Virgin Mary, yields her due respect and honor, and acknowledges this visit an honor undue to herself; yet does she not adore her to God's dishonor; we may neither undervalue God's graces in others, nor idol them for the same. The excellencies of the creatures are so far forth well respected and reverenced, as no interposition thereof clouds God's majesty.

**V. 44. ***{as soon as the voice, &c.}* See note on verse 41.

**V. 45. ***{blessed is she that believed}* By the instinct of the Holy Ghost, she declares wherein the blessedness of Mary (as also of all the elect) consists; that is, true faith apprehending all the promises in Christ, which shall certainly be fulfilled.

**V. 46. ***{My soul doth magnify}* That is, extols and praises: not as hypocrites use in words only, but with the inward affections of my soul. So she attributes all the favors received, to the mere and free mercies of God, leaving nothing to her own merits.

**V. 47. ***{and my spirit hath rejoiced in God}* My soul has an inward joy in God's mercy and salvation: there can be no solid rejoicing until we believe God to be our Savior.

**V. 48. ***{shall call me blessed}* For this favor freely bestowed on me, which all ages shall speak of: she says not, shall call me Queen of Heaven, Lady, etc. much less, shall adore me: see note on verses 28, 42, 43.

**V. 49. ***{for he that is mighty, &c.}* That is, future ages shall so acknowledge me blessed, as attributing all which God has done for me, to his free mercy, not any merit of mine: there can be nothing more injurious to this blessed saint, than sacrilegiously to put on her the spoils taken from Christ her Savior: here is no, *roga patrem, jube natum*, etc. See *Offic. beatæ Mariæ*.

*{and holy is his name}* The name of God sometimes here imports God himself, as he manifests himself to us, see Matthew 6. 9. and *ἅγιον* holy signifies also venerable and sacred, or separated from things vulgar, or profane: so ought we to speak and think of the holy God, with all reverence. See Exod. 20. 7.

**V. 50. ***{his mercy is on them that fear him}* This speech seems taken from the covenant of God: Gen. 17. 7. and Exodus 20. 6. Deuter. 7. 9. Psalm 105. 8. God is ever one, unchangeable, and therefore constant in his favors, as appeared in the example of Abraham, and his seed; but because all are not children of Abraham, who according to the flesh were descended of Abraham, but the faithful and holy only; therefore she here restrains the effect of the promise to those who fear God. See Psalm 103. 17. in vain do they boast that God is their father, who degenerate from the faith, and sanctity of those saints, whom he owned for his children. See 1 Pet. 1. 17. under this word *fear him*, are understood, faith, obedience, and all piety, and true religion.

**V. 51. ***{he hath shewed strength with his arm}* *ἐποίησε* *κράτος*: so Psalm 118. 11. *עשה* *חיל*, *hose chajil*, *ἐποίησε* *δύναμιν*, 70. that is, *δυνατῶς*, powerfully. So Numb. 24. 18. and Israel *עשה* *חיל*, 70. *ἐποίησεν* *ἐν* *ἰσχύϊ*, shall do valiantly, which phrase is sometimes used for the achievement of victories, wherein might and power is showed. So the Syriac interpreter gives the words as if it were *ἐνίκησεν*, he overcame, etc. the arm of the Lord imports his power, commonly in opposition to all other helps, as Isaiah 59. 16. See Psalm 89. 13. Isaiah 51. 9.

*{he hath scattered the proud in, &c.}* God shows his power in descending and lifting up the humble: the proud he scatters in their imaginations and vain counsels, as the wind the chaff: see Psalm 33. 10. 2 Samuel 15. 34. Job 5. 12. 1 Peter 5. 6. Isaiah 8. 10. Gen. 11. 8, 9. Isaiah 14. 13, 14. Ezek. 28. 2, 7.

**V. 52. ***{he hath put down the mighty}* 1 Sam. 2. 6. Job 5. 11. that is, kings, princes, etc. as appears not only in the examples of Nebuchadnezzar, Saul, etc. but many others: and it is the same power and providence of God to exalt and set up whom he pleases, as we see in David, Esther, Joseph, Mordecai, etc. and the blessed Virgin here speaking.

**V. 53. ***{he hath filled the hungry}* See Psal. 34. 10. 1 Samuel 2. 5. in this also she sets out the divine and good providence of God, disposing of all persons, estates, and conditions human.

**V. 54. ***{in remembrance of his mercy}* See Jerem. 31. 3, 20. This is accommodate to man's understanding, who then properly remembers, when he reminds or calls to mind and memory, that which was before out of mind and forgotten: which cannot be properly spoken of God, who cannot forget, the meaning is, that although God might seem, in the long time of Israel's affliction, to have forgotten his promise made to Abraham, and the fathers; concerning the covenant made with them, and concerning their Messiah, yet in the appointed time he makes it appear in the fulfilling thereof, that he never forgot it nor them.

**V. 55. ***{as he spake to our fathers}* Gen. 17. 9, 10. Psalm 132. 11, 12.

*{to his seed for ever}* That is, to the faithful and holy, see Gal. 3. 16, 26, 27, 29.

**V. 56. ***{about three months}* Until her delivery; probably that she might see that fulfilled, which the Angel proposed to her, for the confirmation of her faith.

**V. 57. ***{and she brought forth a son}* So God ever fulfills his promises in good time.

**V. 58. ***{rejoiced with her}* This is that holy sympathy which God commands, Rom. 12. 15.

**V. 59. ***{on the eighth day}* See Gen. 17. 12. Levit. 12. 3. not without an excellent mystery was this sacrament to be given on the eighth day; intimating the absolute circumcision of the heart, and taking away all corruptions of flesh and blood, sins and sinful affections, in the sabbath and rest to come in Christ, Heb. 4. 9.

*{they called him Zacharias}* They who were to circumcise him, as the common custom was, called him by his father's name.

**V. 60. ***{he shall be called John}* It is likely that Zachary had by writing informed Elizabeth concerning the whole vision, and imposition of this name by the Angel. See note on verse 13.

**V. 61. ***{none of thy kindred}* They relied on the custom, which (had their assertion been true) may never be followed against the word and commandment of God.

**V. 62. ***{they made signs to his father}* To end the controversy, they do by signs consult his father.

**V. 63. ***{he asked for}* Or *required*: that is, made signs to have brought him writing tables: so understand, saying, to be writing, his name is John.

**V. 64. ***{his mouth was opened}* His voice and use of speech was restored him again.

*{and praised God}* Doubtless, acknowledging his dumbness, a just punishment of his slowness to believe: the righteous are not ashamed to confess their faults to God's honor: and this served to inform the present, that this child was, (as conceived out of the ordinary course) so promised by an heavenly oracle.

**V. 65. ***{and fear came on all}* That is, a reverend consideration of all these things: so ought we to meditate on God's works, as that they may seriously affect us.

**V. 66. ***{all they that heard}* All, that is, many: possibly many also forgot them: as it commonly comes to pass, in things most observable and divine.

*{the hand of the Lord was with him}* He profited so, as that there appeared a singular power, and manifest grace of God in him.

**V. 67. ***{filled with the Holy Ghost}* Inspired with an excellent measure of the Spirit of prophecy concerning the mystery of our salvation by Christ.

**V. 68. ***{visited and redeemed}* He speaks of that which was speedily to come, as if it were done, to show the certainty of God's counsels and promises: to visit, is out of a care and purpose to relieve, comfort, and help: though God be ever present with us, he does not always manifest it: but when he shows in some eminent effect, that he has care of his people, then he is said to visit them: so also when he punishes, he is said to visit. See Jerem. 14. 10. Amos 3. 2. Psal. 89. 33. Isa. 27. 1. Isa. [24.6]. Here he visited to redeem his people from sin and damnation, which he did by the passion of Christ.

**V. 69. ***{an horn of salvation}* That is, *δύναμιν* *καὶ* *βασιλείαν* *σωτηρίας*, Theophylact, power and a saving kingdom: the Hebrews signified, strength and defense against enemies (Psalm 132. 17, 18. Psalm 89. 17, 13.) by an horn of salvation.

**V. 70. ***{as he spake by the mouth of his holy Prophets}* Who living in divers ages, yet with one consent taught concerning salvation by Christ only: this doctrine was from the beginning: God spoke by the mouth of all his Prophets, Heb. 1. 1. 2 Pet. 1. 21. See Chap. 24. 44.

*{since the world began}* As Acts 3. 21. and Psal. 119. 52. *מעולם*, *meholam*, which the 70. give *ἀπ᾽* *αἰῶνος*, (the word here used) of old, or from the beginning of time: from the first time that God began to speak by any Prophet. Sometimes the same word imports eternity.

**V. 71. ***{That we should be saved, &c.}* This declares the end of Christ's coming: this salvation we must understand to be from our spiritual enemies; Satan and sin, with whom we have truceless war: see Ephes. 6. 12, etc.

**V. 72. ***{To remember his holy covenant}* Because in the long affliction of his people, his promise might have seemed forgotten; but God ever remembers and in the appointed time performs his promises in Christ: has he promised remission of sins? he performs it in Christ. Has he promised eternal life? he performs it in Christ: therefore was the book of the law (containing all God's promises) sprinkled with the blood of the sacrifice (Exodus 24. 7, 8. Hebrews 9. 19.) which signified the blood of Christ, in whom all these promises were fulfilled.

**V. 73. ***{and the oath which, &c.}* See Gen. 12. 3. Gen. 17. 4. Gen. 22. 16. Heb. 6. 13. That is, his covenant which he confirmed with an oath; the Syriac gives it—*juramenti quod juravit Abraha*. See Hebrews 6. 13, 14, 16, 17, 18. Genes. 22. 16. and 17, etc.

**V. 74. ***{that we being delivered}* The efficient cause of our deliverance and safety is God's free mercy: the final our serving him in holiness. See 1 Thes. 4. 7. 1 Cor. 6. 20. Titus 2. 11. Rom. 12. 1. Ephes. 1. 3. We are delivered from servile fear, (Rom. 8. 15.) in our peace with God through faith apprehending the merit of Christ for our justification, Rom. 5. 1. without which none can rightly serve God: they who in doubtful anxiety of mind know not whether God accepts their service or no, can never be accepted: for without faith it is impossible to please him, who loves willing and cheerful sacrifice: first then the conscience must be quieted in assurance of God's mercy, that we may serve him, as David begging a merciful audience for his prayer, says, *if thou Lord shouldest mark iniquities: O Lord, who shall stand? but there is forgivenesse with thee, that thou mayest be feared*: Psalm 130. 3, 4. when God gives us peace of conscience; he sweetly invites us to come unto him cheerfully, and to call upon him in our wants, with a filial confidence: as Romans 8. 15, 16. whereas, without hope of attaining, who can pray cheerfully? *Tolle Gubernatori spem perveniendi, & in mediis fluctibus incertus errabit*; take away from the master of the ship hope of safe arrival, and being uncertain, he will let his vessel go adrift, whither wind and tide will carry her. Now for the present, our freedom from fear, is as our immunity from sin, not but that some Canaanites are still left to exercise us: but in that we are secure of victory in Christ. See Rom. 7. 25. 1 Cor. 15. 57.

**V. 75. ***{in holiness}* See 1 Pet. 1. 15, 16, 17. We must be constant all our life: it is not enough to begin well, we must serve him in holiness towards him, who searches the hearts, and righteousness and equity in just dealing with men, who are witnesses of our actions: before them also our light must shine, to God's glory, Matth. 5. 16.

*{before him}* Before the all-seeing God, to whom no hypocrite can be acceptable. See Psal. 16. 8.

**V. 76. ***{goe before the face}* See Mal. 3. 1. He was the voice of the crier, to prepare the way, and proclaim the presence of the King of kings, John 1. 23. the friend of the bridegroom Christ, John 3. 29. the burning and shining light, (Matth. 11. 9, 10, 11. John 5. 35.) born before Christ: and as Nazianzen *orat.* 42. says, *τῷ* *προδρόμῳ* *λύχνῳ* *τὸ* *φῶς* *ἀκολουθεῖ* *τὸ* *ὑπέρλαμπρον*, that most excellent light (Christ) follows (John) the foregoing light.

**V. 77. ***{to give knowledge of salvation}* This is to prepare the way of the Lord: to teach them the will of God, concerning the true pardon of their sins in Christ, which is the sum of the Gospel.

**V. 78. ***{the dayspring}* The Sun of righteousness Christ Jesus, Mal. 4. 2. the world’s sun rises from the low horizon; but Christ descended from the incomprehensible height of majesty, far above all creatures, to give us light of salvation: even from the Father: (John 3. 13.) as the beams from the sun; he had not his rising from the earth, where (as man) he was conceived and born: see John 16. 28.

**V. 79. ***{To give light}* Or *to appear to*: see Isa. 9. 1. Mat. 4. 16. 2 Pet. 1. 19. until Christ our light shine in our hearts, there can be nothing but deathful ignorance therein.

*{in the way of peace}* That is, salvation and true happiness, in our peace with God, and peace of conscience.

**V. 80. ***{strong in spirit}* In the gifts and graces of the Spirit: whereby it appeared that God prepared him to a singular office: see Chap. 2. 40, 52.

*{in the deserts}* The places of Judea which were least inhabited; as are great forests: the rough mountainous country, as verse 40. See 1 Kings 2. 34. 2 Kings 3. 8. Joshua 15. 61. not that John Baptist lived like an eremite, from all society of men, but in some obscure places, near Jordan: (as appears Luke 3. 3.) until he was called to preach repentance, and to baptize. See Matth. 3. 1, etc. Luke 3. 1, etc. that he might do that office to which God assigned him, in manifesting and declaring Christ now exhibited to Israel, John 1. 31.

## CHAPTER 2

**Verse 1. ***{In those days}* Some say in the forty-second year of Augustus Caesar, Emperor of Rome, in the thirty-third year of Herod the Great, son of Antipater: (Epiphanius *Hæres.* 51. N. 22.) then came the promised Shiloh, the Messiah, the King of peace: that Jacob's prophecy might appear fulfilled, Gen. 49. 9. in that the scepter and kingly power was now departed from Judah; as also that his kingdom was spiritual, not of this world, to whose princes he would be tributary, and subject, thereby declaring, that he came not to take away the order of their government, but to establish it.

*{all the world}* All that which is subject to the Romans; these western parts of the world.

**V. 2. ***{this taxing was first made}* This, *first*, relates to other taxes following, whether under Cyrenius, as some think, or others: *ἀπογραφὴ*; is here, taking in writing, the poll or name of every region, city, family, and person, that they might tax, or cess them by the head: this taxing was first made, when Augustus Caesar, being desirous to know the wealth and power of his empire; (though perhaps not with any present purpose of levying moneys thereby) among others gave commission to Cyrenius to tax Syria.

*{when Cyrenius was Governor of Syria}* That is, Sulpitius Quirinus. See Josephus *Antiq.* lib. 17. cap. 15. and lib. 18. cap. 1. *was Governor*, *ἡγεμονεύοντος*, governing: there were other governors in ordinary, to whom this commission of taxing was not given by Caesar; but to Quirinus or Cyrenius, *jure extraordinario*: the Emperors assigned great and chief men of the Empire to this office: so Drusus, this Quirinus, and others were commissionated by Augustus, so the great Germanicus, by Tiberius, Tacit. *Annal. Libro primo*, etc. therefore: this *ἡγεμονία*, and *ἡγεμονεύων*, is used to import, any great power and authority in the Commonwealth: therefore in the Scripture sometimes the Roman Emperor is called *ἡγεμών*: so sometimes, it signifies Caesar's Deputy, see Matthew 27. 2, etc. So that Cyrenius was not now President of Syria in ordinary, as afterward, after Archelaus, when he was made Governor, both to govern that province, and to tax and levy moneys on them; whereof you may read more in Suetonius, and Dio. *Roman. hist.*

**V. 3. ***{into his own city}* That is, into the chief town or city of their own tribe: as the metropolis or chief city of Benjamin, was Jerusalem; of Judah, Bethlehem: thither came Joseph and Mary, though they dwelt at Nazareth of Galilee: herein appears the admirable providence of God, (*who doth according to his will in the army of Heaven, and among the inhabitants of the earth*, Daniel 4. 35.) by a secret instinct moving the mind of Caesar dreaming of nothing less, to give that very command which must occasion the fulfilling God's Word by the Prophet concerning Christ the Messiah's birth at Bethlehem-Judah, so men shall do God's will when they intend it not.

**V. 4. ***{city of David}* The city where David, Joseph, and Christ was born, of the lineage of David. See John 7. 42. Mic. 5. 2. Mat. 2. 5. 1 Sam. 16. 1.

**V. 5. ***{his espoused wife}* Chapter 1. 27. she is called a virgin espoused, and here an espoused wife: which is to show that Joseph had (according to the Angel's word) taken her to wife, Matthew 1. 24. who was before betrothed to him. For *espousal* is but marriage promised, or contracted: and marriage is espousal accomplished: therefore she is here called his espoused wife, because he had married her: he had not known her. Matthew 1. 25.

*{great with child}* See Matth. 1. 18.

**V. 6. ***{while they were there}* While Joseph and Mary were at Bethlehem, to be taxed, for such was the great multitude of people to be taxed, and to give in their names to the officers thereto authorized, that they could not be presently dispatched and dismissed; but here was the secret providence of God disposing of all things so, as that the truth of his promise concerning the place of Christ's nativity, might appear fulfilled.

*{the days were accomplished}* The time was come that Mary should be delivered; thus God used the command of Caesar, and issuing out of warrants by his officers, to summon the people to appear before them in their several cities to which they belonged, to that very end which himself had proposed, and many ages before declared by the Prophet, that Christ the Messiah of the seed of David should be born at Bethlehem the city of David: men pursue their own designs, ignorant of that which God will bring to pass thereby.

**V. 7. ***{her firstborn}* He is said to be the firstborn, before whom was none elder by the same parents, although he be the only son, after whom there is no second born, as Theophylact well observes.

*{no room for them}* With so great a confluence of people coming to be taxed, all the rooms of the inn were taken up; only they found room in a stable.

**V. 8. ***{keeping watch, &c.}* *ἀγραυλοῦντες*, that is, (as Theophylact expresses it) *ἐν* *τῷ* *ἀγρῷ* *αὐλιζόμενοι* *καὶ* *διάγοντες*, lying or being without in the fields, intending their flocks which were otherwise in danger of wild beasts, or thieves. Some would hence conclude against the ancient custom of the Church, observing the nativity of Christ on the twenty-fifth of December; supposing it improbable that shepherds would in so cold a part of the year abide with their flocks in the fields; not considering the diversities of air according to the diversity of climates, neither the vigilant industry of countrymen, when necessity requires. The learned Is. Casaubon, *Exercit.* 2. *ad Annal. Eccles. B. An.* 1. *Num.* 14. well advises, *Quare vetusta traditio Ecclesiæ non temere nobis rejicienda*, it being but a cold argument which can be grounded on this supposition, that the shepherds would watch their flocks only in summer; the same hazard and necessity of feeding being also in winter, if not greater.

**V. 9. ***{came upon them}* The Syriac interpreter gives it, came unto them: *ἐπέστη* *αὐτοῖς*. It imports such an apparition as suddenly came upon them, and unexpectedly, they little thinking of any such thing; that it might appear to be no compact.

*{glory of the Lord}* Such an heavenly brightness and light that the amazed shepherds might by so unusual glory, perceive that he was the Angel of the Lord: thus God adorned his messenger, to the end, the message by him sent, might deeply affect their minds, and be received no otherwise, than as from the mouth of the Lord. Had the Angel appeared with less majesty and authority (as sometimes Angels appeared) perhaps this great mystery would not have been so readily believed; we more easily credit that which we believe comes from heaven: so that this glory was here necessary for the confirmation of these witnesses' faith; who were to find the Messiah in so low an estate of his humiliation.

**V. 10. ***{fear not}* As if he had said; I am not sent unto you from an angry judge, nor with any sad message, but with joyful news: so said the Angel to Zachary, chap. 1. 13. *Fear not.* In great and sudden fears, the mind cannot be attentive to that which is spoken, therefore the Angel first allays their fear, that they may be enabled to make a right use of his message.

*{great joy which shall be to all people}* The greatest joy which ever sounded in the ears of men or Angels, as concerning a true and eternal blessedness in Christ: and that which concerns all the people of God, of all nations; all which are, or shall be, Jews and Gentiles, bond and free: all believers.

**V. 11. ***{for unto you is born this day, &c.}* For your good and salvation. So Isaiah 9. 6. *unto us a child is born, unto us a son is given, &c.* this is that great mystery of godliness, *God manifested in the flesh*, 1 Timothy 3. 16. *this day*, after so many ages, foretold by the Prophets, signified by the legal ceremonies, expected by the faithful; in this afflicted condition of Israel, now he is come: this day he is born: *this is the day which the Lord hath made, we will rejoyce and be glad in it*, Psalm 118. 24.

*{in the city of David}* In Bethlehem, where God promised his nativity, Micha 5. 2.

*{a Saviour}* See Mat. 21. 1. *Christ the Lord*, the promised Messiah, both signified anointed: the only Mediator between God and man, Christ our Savior, God and man: the Angel's Lord and preserver, and man's Redeemer.

**V. 12. ***{this shall be a sign}* That they might not be offended and think themselves deluded, when they should find Christ the Savior in so poor a worldly condition, the Angel prevents that beforehand, showing them how they should find him.

**V. 13. ***{with the angel a multitude}* For the more evident confirmation of true faith by so many witnesses, and their faith by so glorious a company.

*{of the heavenly host}* The Hebrews used to call companies of Angels *מחנה* *אלהים* *machane elohim*, Gen. 32. 2. 70 *παρεμβολὴ* *θεοῦ*; the tents, or host of God: and *צבא* *השמים* *tseba hasshamaiim*, the host of Heaven, 1 Kings 22. 19. and he is called the Lord of Hosts, Psalm 24. etc.

*{praising God}* Thus God would have this public service (for so their multitude made it) performed by all that have knowledge of, and interest in the birth of Christ: as to manifest his mercy and truth, so to condemn their stupid ingratitude, who perversely refuse to join in this duty: as if general precepts in God's word, right reason, and the examples of men and Angels inspired by God's Spirit, the chosen witnesses of our Savior's nativity, and for this same purpose sent by God himself from Heaven, that they might thus celebrate that day, were not sufficient warrant to lead them to imitation, and joining in a holy harmony with them.

**V. 14. ***{Glory to God}* Let God be glorified, in man's reconciliation to him. Or they congratulate and rejoice praising God for both these.

*{in the highest}* In Heaven; Hebr. *במרום* *bemmarom*, on high, Psal. 93. 4. So the Syriac hence has *במרומא* (*in excelsis*) *bamraume*.

*{on earth peace}* See on Luke 19. 38. and at this time all the world was at peace. By peace the Hebrews also signified, prosperity, health, and salvation, here was peace come, (and declared by the Angel) to the inhabitants of the world, through Christ now born; therefore verse 11. he is called, the Savior. See Col. 1. 13, 14. 20. this peace is here principally meant.

*{good will towards men}* The free mercy of God towards man and love and good will of one man towards another: whereas sinful man was at enmity with God; in Christ he is reconciled. See Rom. 5. 10. Coloss. 1. 20, 21.

**V. 15. ***{the shepherds}* Gr. *the men*: *shepherds*, that is, the men which were shepherds.

*{and see this thing}* *τὸ* *ῥῆμα* *τοῦτο*, this word: so the Hebrews put *דבר* *dabar*, a word, *pro rebus verbo celebratis*, as was noted, chap. 1. 37. for the things spoken, 1 Kings 17. 9, 21. etc. So here, this word signifies, this thing which the Angel told us. See verse 19. and on Deut. chap. 1. 37.

**V. 16. ***{came with haste}* Neither the hazard of their flocks, length of way, nor darkness retarded them: this faith and ready obedience, God rewarded, in their finding the Savior, to their greater joy and comfort, verse 20.

**V. 17. ***{they made known abroad the saying}* This was their faith, and sincerity, that they truly related to others those things which they had seen, and heard of the Angel concerning Christ. Thus would God propagate the glorious mysteries of the kingdom of Christ, by men so obscure and simple; to beat down the arrogance of carnal reason, so to confirm our faith. For, as *Lactantius* says of Christian truth, *abfuit ab iis fingendi voluntas, & astutia, quoniam rudes fuerunt*, etc. lib. 5. chap. 3. they had neither will nor subtlety to feign, because they were ignorant: therefore they were of the mind of those four Lepers, 2 Kings 7. 8, 9. being themselves refreshed and abundantly enriched by the *Assyrian* tents, they said one to another, *We do not well, this day is a day of good tidings, and we hold our peace,—Now therefore come, that we may go and tell*, etc.

**V. 18. ***{wondered at those things}* As thoughtful, what all these things would come to. This knowledge therefore left them more inexcusable, who thereby did not profit in the faith of Christ.

**V. 19. ***{kept all these things}* *τὰ* *ῥήματα*: that is, as *Theophylact* interprets *τὰ* *πράγματα*. See on verse 15. those things, the speech of the Angel, and all that which the shepherds related, and all that which now came to pass.

*{and pondered them in her heart}* Diligently comparing, and laying these things together.

**V. 20. ***{the shepherds returned}* To their calling and habitations.

*{glorifying and praising, &c.}* This is an effect of faith, and the end of our coming to Christ, and of all that spiritual and secular blessing, which God bestows on us. See Psalm 50. 15. 1 Cor. 10. 31.

**V. 21. ***{when the eight days were accomplished}* See Gen. 17. 12. Levit. 12. 3. Luke 1. 59. Thus would he be subjected to the law, that he might redeem those who were under the law, (Gal. 4. 4.) and deliver us from the curse of the law.

*{his name was called Jesus}* The Jews used to name their children when they circumcised them, as we do when we baptize them: God would have him named, according to the office assigned him; to declare that there is no other name under Heaven whereby we can be saved, Acts 4. 12. Neither was this done by the counsel of man but God, who sent his Angel to name him so before his incarnation. See 1. 21. Luke 1. 31.

**V. 22. ***{days of her purification}* See Levit. 12. 2, 5, 6. For though Mary was not made unclean, in her conception, but rather sanctified by the Holy Ghost and power of the Most High; yet thus ought they then to fulfill the law; which was not as yet repealed, or taken away, as it was afterward, when Christ had accomplished and fulfilled the same.

*{to present him to the Lord}* To dedicate, commend him to, or set him before the Lord in the place of his public worship. See Exod. 13. 12. Numb. 18. 15. Exod. 22. 29.

**V. 23. ***{every male}* This was he for whose sake all the firstborn under the Law were holy to the Lord, as *Tertullian* says, c. 23. *De carne Christi*.

**V. 24. ***{according to that which is said}* Levit. 12. 2. 6.

*{a pair of turtle doves}* Which offering was appointed unto them who were so poor, that they were not able to offer a Lamb, or a Sheep, Levit. 5. 7.

**V. 25. ***{was just and devout}* One that feared God, and expected the coming of the Messiah, see verse 38. and Mark 15. 43.

*{the Holy Ghost was upon him}* That is, the spirit of prophecy, this is here inserted, that we may know, that this testimony of Simeon concerning Christ, was not human, but divine, and the very dictate of the Holy Ghost by him.

**V. 26. ***{that he should not see death}* That he should not die, before he had seen Christ the anointed King, Priest, and Prophet, come in the flesh. See Psal. 2. 6. Isa. 61. 1. Dan. 9. 24.

**V. 27. ***{came by the Spirit}* By the secret instinct and motion of the Holy Ghost, he came not now into the temple, casually, or called, or invited thither by any counsel or command of man, but by the motion of God's Spirit, who had before revealed to him, that he should not die before he had seen the Lord's Christ, the promised and long-expected Messiah come into the world.

*{when the parents brought the child Jesus}* That is Joseph (the reputed father of Jesus: See Heb. 7. 3.) and Mary his mother.

**V. 28. ***{then took he him up}* With great joy did he embrace Christ in his arms, and blessed God: that is, gave thanks and praise to God for Christ, now born in the flesh, which the Spirit of God had likewise revealed unto him: for Christ in his state of humiliation was, as it were, *sol in nube*, not known by the testimony of carnal eyes to be the Christ, the Savior—*no man can say, that Jesus is the Lord, but by the Holy Ghost*, 1 Cor. 12. 3.

**V. 29. ***{Lord now lettest thou thy servant depart, &c.}* Now that you have shown me the fulfilling of your promise concerning my Savior the Messiah, whom you have given to raise up the tribes of Jacob, to restore the preserved of Israel, and a light to lighten the Gentiles to be your salvation unto the end of the earth, Isa. 49. 6. Heb. 2. 10. & 5. 9. now I willingly and cheerfully leave this present life.

**V. 30. ***{for mine eyes have seen thy salvation}* Psal. 98. Isa. 52. 10. he did before this time see Christ with the eye of faith, as Abraham did, and rejoiced: but now he saw with his bodily eyes that fulfilled which he before expected.

**V. 31. ***{before the face of all people}* As that which you will make manifest to all nations; but thus he speaks after the manner of the Prophets, concerning that which was future, as if it were present, for the infallible certainty thereof: Christ was not yet, but some time after revealed, by the preaching of the gospel, unto the nations; but he thus speaks, as of that of which he was fully assured, in its time should come to pass.

**V. 32. ***{a light to lighten the Gentiles}* A light to shine unto the Gentiles who were aliens to the covenant of God, and sat in the region and shadow of death, Matthew 4. 16. that is, ignorance and spiritual blindness: now shall he teach them the knowledge of God, and life everlasting: he alludes to Psalm 98. 2. Isaiah 9. 2. Isaiah 42. 6. and 49. 6. this is that which God promised by all the Prophets, the sum of whose words is, that all Nations of the world should look up to Christ, the only author of salvation. See John 8. 12. Coloss. 3. 5. Ephes. 5. 3. 1 Corinth. 6. 4. Acts 26. 16. Isaiah 60. 1. etc.

*{the glory of thy people Israel}* With whom he had a covenant; who were his adopted and firstborn, Exod. 4. 22. to them peculiarly was Christ promised: among them was he born in the flesh: see Rom. 1. 3. Rom. 9. 5. to them he first offered the Covenant of free grace in him.

**V. 33. ***{marvelled}* Not because they believed not (being confirmed by so many testimonies of the Angels, Shepherds, and Wise men) but because they apprehended an admirable concurrence of all these, declaring the truth of God fulfilled in Christ.

**V. 34. ***{Simeon blessed them}* He prayed for the success of Christ's kingdom, and Joseph's and Mary's prosperity.

*{behold this child is set for the fall, &c.}* This *κεῖται*, *is set*, imports not only the event, but also the decree of God: which when it is come to the execution thereof; then, as we must understand, that the free grace of God (calling and working faith in the Elect to apprehend the merits of Christ for their justification) comes between the decree of saving them, and their salvation: so, between the fall of Reprobates, and God's decree concerning them, their own corruption, and willful unbelief intercedes: so that as God justly decreed to punish them for that which he foresaw in them from eternity: So does he no otherwise execute that his counsel in time, than by a just punishment of their wickedness; and they bear no more than that which themselves have caused, and truly deserved: and so understand this place, That Christ was set or appointed for the fall and greater condemnation of unbelievers (who willfully fall upon this rock of offense which God laid in Sion) and for the resurrection and recovery of the Elect to whom he gives faith, Ephes. 2. 8. So God saves the Elect in free mercy, and casts away the Reprobate in absolute justice, whom he predestinated to just punishment, but not to sin, but for sin. See Isaiah 8. 14, 15. Rom. 9. 32. 1 Pet. 2. 8.

*{which shall be spoken against}* By unbelievers and wicked men, the enemies of Christ and the Gospel; who, however dissenting among themselves, yet all combine against him, as if he were the only mark for all the powers of Hell to shoot at.

**V. 35. ***{a sword shall pierce}* That is, bitter sorrows shall afflict your heart: he seems to allude to Psalm 105. 18. This he says concerning Mary's heart-grief which she should conceive for her dear Son's sufferings, to which she was conformable in a double capacity: first as a tender mother: next as a true member of his mystical body wherein his sufferings were meritorious, and sanctified hers.

**V. 36. ***{A prophetess}* That is much exercised in the holy Scriptures, and inspired of the Holy Ghost to declare things hidden, extraordinary, and unusual. See 1 Sam. 18. 10. or things to come. See Acts 11. 28. 1 Cor. 14. 3. Judg. 4. 4. 2 Kings 22. 14. this Luke inserts to show the authority by which she spoke, not as a vulgar woman; and to the same end serves the description of her.

**V. 37. ***{departed not from the temple}* Not that she was a Recluse: but this shows that she daily frequented the temple: *night and day*, or *continually*, in such acts of Religion, import a constancy, without ceasing, not without intermission. See 1 Thess. 5. 17. 1 Tim. 5. 5. Luke 24. 53. 1 Thess. 3. 10.

**V. 38. ***{At the same instant}* Gr. *the same hour*: see Matthew 9. 22.

*{gave thanks likewise}* Gr. *answered in confession*: confessed accordingly, as Simeon had done; agreeing in her testimony with his, who had professed Christ, now presented before the Lord, to be the promised Savior.

*{and spake of him to all}* She spoke of him to all them who, frequenting the temple, expected salvation by Christ: those now heard what Anna said, and probably she spoke the same elsewhere as occasion served.

*{that looked for redemption}* (Mark 15. 43. and here verse 25.) As now at hand, and at the door: though there was a fearful apostasy in Israel, and depravation of Religion, yet still God had his Church among them, some who expected salvation by Christ.

**V. 39. ***{They returned into Galilee}* That is, Joseph and Mary with the child Jesus, after they had been in Egypt, as Joseph was warned by the Angel, which story Matthew records, Mat. 2. 13. etc. and this Evangelist omits in pursuit of the following History; for as *Epiphanius. Alog. haeres.* 51. 6. well observes; God gave every Evangelist his proper task: so as that they related some things *συμφώνως* *καὶ* *ἴσως* with one accord and equally; thereby showing that they all drew out of the same fountain: other things omitted of all the rest, one of them rehearses, as the Holy Spirit gave him that for his part, as has been noted.

**V. 40. ***{Filled with wisdom}* With admirable wisdom showing itself in him as he grew up: this was the effect of the Deity dwelling in him, shining from under the veil of his assumed humanity; to the Deity could nothing be added: an infinite cannot increase: in the humanity he was as we in all things, without sin, Hebr. 4. 15. and so had a native ignorance of some things; from, which he grew as he increased in age: for though the Deity and humanity were but one person, yet were not the incommunicable properties of the Deity given to the human nature: the Deity was infinite, omnipresent, etc. so was not the humanity, etc.

**V. 41. ***{The passover}* See Mat. 26. 2. and Luke 22. 1. they went up to the Passover as was commanded, Deut. 16. 1. It seems Mary came up of her own free devotion, as did also Hanna, 1 Sam. 1. 2. etc.

**V. 43. ***{Fulfilled the days}* The days of the feast prescribed by the Law, Exod. 23. 15. That is, the feast of Passover, which began the fourteenth day of the first month at even, when the Paschal Lamb was to be slain, in remembrance of their deliverance out of Egypt, Deut. 16. 3. as also to prefigure Christ our Passover (1 Cor. 5. 7.) the Lamb that takes away the sins of the world, John 1. 29. to be slain in the Evening of time, Acts 2. 17. Hebr. 1. 2. 1 Pet. 1. 20. 1 John 2. 18. which we are to remember by the Supper of the Lord, 1 Cor. 11. 25, 26. until he come again. Now albeit this feast of Passover was but one day in the year to be kept; yet hereunto were joined the seven days, the feast of unleavened bread immediately following the same; which began the fifteenth day of the same month: This veiled many excellent mysteries. 1. The seven days figured the whole time of our life, wherein we must eat the bread of affliction, Deut. 16. 3. Gen. 3. 19. Acts 14. 22. 2 Tim. 3. 11, 12. 2. We must all our lifetime thankfully remember our deliverance by Christ, our Passover. 3. We must purge out the old leaven of malice and wickedness, not only for some few days, but all our days, that we may keep the feast with *the unleavened bread of sincerity and truth*, 1 Cor. 5. 8.

*{the child Jesus tarried behind}* Not out of any disobedience, but to show what his office was, to which he was designed by his Father; the Prophets foretold hereof, Isaiah 61. 1. and Christ mentioned it; chap. 4. 43.

**V. 44. ***{To have been in the company}* *ἐν* *τῇ* *συνοδίᾳ* the Syriac Interpreter gives *cum comitibus eorum* with the company or companions in the way, for they used to come up to the feasts at Jerusalem from all parts of the land in great companies; and so to return home again.

**V. 45. ***{They returned back}* Not without great perplexity of mind; that not only for loss of time, labor, and the company of their friends and kindred, but principally for fear of the child Jesus.

**V. 46. ***{The midst of the Doctors}* With the Doctors: it is an Hebraism: as c. 22. 27. it may be he sat at their feet, not in the chief seats; of which Luke 11. 43.

**V. 47. ***{Were astonished}* *ἐξίσταντο* properly signifies, they were out of themselves with admiration; it was beyond their understandings which they heard, in the preludium, and first relish of Christ's ministry: the same word used c. 24. 22. *Budaeus* interprets, *ad stuporem usque eos perculerunt*.

**V. 48. ***{Thy father and I}* *Theophylact* thinks she thus calls him in respect of the Jews, *ἵνα* *μὴ* *νομισθῇ* *ἐκ* *πορνείας* *εἶναι*, etc.; but indeed she thus called him, because though he was not his natural father, yet was he (as *Epiphanius* says. *Ebionit. haeres.* 30.) *ἐν* *τάξει*, that is, *vice*, or *munere*, a father; the Evangelist best expresses it, his supposed father, c. 3. 23.

**V. 49. ***{My father's business}* *ἐν* *τοῖς* *τοῦ* *πατρός* *μου*—in the things which are my father's: the Syriac gives it, *know ye not that I must be*, *דבית* *אבי* *debeth abbi*; *in my father's house?* that is, the temple where they found him; the meaning is, doing that which appertained to man's salvation: his purpose was to show, that though duty to parents must be observed, yet duty to God must be preferred, Mat. 10. 37.

**V. 50. ***{They understood not the saying}* For his office of a Mediator and Savior was not yet known to them, though the Angel had foretold it, Mat. 1. 21. and Zachary had prophesied it, Luke 1. 68. etc.

**V. 51. ***{Was subject unto them}* From that time till he was about thirty years old, c. 3. 23. Mark 6. 3. this subjection was not *infirmitatis*, but *pietatis*, as *Ambros. in Luc. l.* 2. c. 2. observes, *disce quid parentibus tuis debeas*, says the same, you owe to your mother's trouble, sorrow, and danger, in bringing you into the world, who when she has brought forth what she long desired, *partu absolvitur, non timore*; she is delivered of the birth, not her fear: many labors and cares of Parents belong to the education of children; ought not you for all these, to requite them with your obedience?

**V. 52. ***{Increased in wisdom}* As a child *lege humanae naturae* as *Euthymius* says well, he increased by little and little; for as God, he was absolutely perfect: because he assumed a reasonable soul as well as a human body; the Deity thereto united was so veiled, that the graces thereof showed themselves by certain degrees, according to the maturity of his human faculties; and so he *increased in wisdom*; though the fullness of the Godhead dwell in him, Coloss. 3. 9.

*{and stature}* *ἡλικία* In stature of body, the Gr. word also signifies the age. So he, as man increased in body and soul; *Nam sicut carnis est aetate proficere, sic est animae sapientia & gratia profecisse.* *Fulgent. de myst. Mediat. ad Thras.* lib. 1.

## CHAPTER 3

**Verse 1. ***{Of Tiberius Caesar}* This was successor of Augustus Caesar, in whose reign Christ was born, chap. 2. 1. after Julius, and Augustus Caesar, the following Emperors were styled Caesars, as the Egyptian Kings were called Pharaohs, and Ptolemies. See *Alexand. ab Alexandr. Genial. dier.* lib. 1. cap. 2. Mark the admirable candor and diligence of the Evangelist in historifying the circumstances of our Savior's Nativity, and entrance into his public ministry, that the truth thereof may evidently appear: he tells in what place, what Emperor's reign, and who were witnesses, etc.

*{Pilate being governor}* Or *Deputy*, appointed by the Roman Emperor to govern that Province.

*{Herod being Tetrarch, &c.}* See on Mat. 14. 1. This was Herod Antipas. *Joseph. de bel. Jud.* l. 2. c. 4. whom Tiberius made Tetrarch of Galilee.

*{of Ituraea, &c.}* Hereof read *Joseph. de bel. Jud.* lib. 2. c. 4. & lib. 3. cap. 2. *Adricomius. Theatr.*: *ter.* s. *Neptalim.* 59. 60. it is a mountainous Region at the foot or mount Libanus.

*{Trachonitis}* This country says *Jerome de locis Hebraicis*, is by the Desert of Arabia, which joins to Bostra. *Josephus de bel. Jud.* lib. 2. chap. 4. calls it Trachon: it is a great Region beyond Jordan, lying between Palestine and Coelesyria. See also *Plin. nat. hist.* l. 5. c. 6.

*{of Abilene}* This country *Adricomius Theat. Neptalim.* 4. says, took its name from Abila, Abyla, or Abela, a famous city of Coelesyria near mount Libanus. *Jerom de loc. Hebraic.* mentions three Towns and Cities in several countries called Abela; this Evangelist would not omit *Lysanias Tetrarchie* because many Jews dwelt therein, in the borders of Decapolis, whence many came to hear Christ, Mat. 4. 25. Mark 5. 20. & 7. 31. See *Is. Casaubon. exercit.* 13. 3.

**V. 2. ***{Annas and Caiaphas being the high priests}* There were three degrees of ministers under the Law; Priests, Levites, and Nethinim: over these the high Priest was chief; and that office was successively tied to the line of Aaron's firstborn, all the rest of his posterity were Priests: but there was to be but one high Priest, at whose death another was still to succeed: that two are here named high Priests together; some think that the Grecian Kings, at their pleasure made high Priests to officiate by the year, not for life. V. *Shindler lex. Pent.* in *כהן* others impute this disorder and violation of God's Law concerning the perpetuity of this office in one man during life, to the power and covetousness of the Romans. But for better resolution we must consider that the high Priest had also his suffragan, or *סגן* *sagan*, who in case of the high Priest's pollution, performed his office: Such was Zephaniah, Jer. 52. 24. the high Priest himself was called *כהן* *הראש* *cohen harosh*: *ἱερεὺς* *ὁ* *μέγας* 2 Kings 22. 8. and *ἱερεὺς* *πρῶτος*, 2 King. 25. 18. the first or chief high Priest: or *כהן* *הגדול* *cohen hagadol*, *the great high Priest*; the suffragan, who also was called high Priest, was styled *כהן* *משנה* *cohen mishne*. *ἱερεὺς* *ὁ* *δεύτερος* 2 Kings 25. 18. or as some read, *υἱὸς* *τῆς* *δευτερώσεως*, as we give *the second Priest*, Jer. 52. 24. The second priest, that is, suffragan or vicar, so was Caiaphas here suffragan to Annas his father-in-law, who was the high Priest; but the Evangelist, John 18. 24. after their vulgar manner of speaking, calls him *τὸν* *Ἀρχιερέα*. See *Jos. Scaliger. Proleg. in Euseb. Is. Casaubon. Exercit.* 13. 4. and D. *Godwin. Moses and Aaron.* l. 1. c. 4. See note on John 18. 13.

*{the words of God}* That is a special revelation and command to execute his office, Luke 1. 80. without which he might not have baptized. See John 1. 33. see also Hebr. 5. 4, 5. I would the Anabaptists would thoroughly examine their own consciences, who sent them to re-baptize; and that they would no longer deceive others, and themselves.

**V. 3. ***{And he came}* Out of the desert into the inhabited places, that he might execute his charge in preaching repentance unto the people for the remission of sins. See Mat. 3. 1. Mark 1. 4.

**V. 4. ***{The voice of one crying, &c.}* Isai. 40. 3. See on Mat. 3. 3. and on John 1. 23.

**V. 5. ***{Every valley shall be filled}* All impediments shall be taken away, so that the way of God and eternal life by Christ, shall be plain and evident to him that believes; which by the works of the Law, as it was obscure under those shadows, so also impassable by reason of the infirmity and corruption of flesh and blood. Rom. 3. 20. Gal. 3. 11, 12. Rom. 9. Rom. 8. 2, 3.

**V. 6. ***{All flesh shall see}* See chap. 2. 30. All sorts of men to whom Christ the Savior of the world shall be preached and revealed. See Psal. 98. 2. Isai. 52. 10. Acts 10. 35.

**V. 7. ***{generation of vipers}* See on Mat. 3. 7. That is, as *Gregory hom.* 20. interprets, *Malae soboles malorum parentum actionem imitantes*, wicked children imitating their wicked parents.

*{to flee from, &c.}* See on Mat. 3. 7.

**V. 8. ***{bring forth fruits}* See on Mat. 3. 8.

*{Abraham to our father}* See on Mat. 3. 9. Which prerogative, similitude of manners may rather claim, *Quam ordo majorum* says *Ambros. in Luc.* lib. 2. cap. 3. than order of parentage.

*{of these stones}* See on Mat. 3. 9. Gregory understands by stones, *cordia Gentilium*, the insensible hearts of the Gentiles, not apprehending the things of God, *Hom.* 20. So *Ambros. in Luc.* l. 2. c. 3. understands here *aedificationem ecclesiae*, the edification of the Church of Christ, consisting of *living stones*, 1 Pet. 2. 5. and those principally of the Gentiles to be converted.

**V. 9. ***{The axe is laid to the root of the trees}* See on Mat. 3. 10. the metaphor showed them, that the instruments of their destruction (such as were Titus and Vespasian) were near, and should quickly execute; except they repented: the axe is laid to the root, *cum tota simul progenies cum parente tollitur.* *Greg. hom.* 20. when the whole progeny is cut up with the parent: which judgment then threatened the Jewish nation, and long it was not, before it came upon them.

**V. 10. ***{What shall we do?}* Their hearts being touched, with John's preaching they inquire (as Peter's hearers also did Acts 2. 37.) what they should do? as if they said, what fruits must we bring forth, that we may flee from the anger to come, having heard the severe doctrine by him delivered concerning their destruction, and the remedy to prevent the same, by him also proposed; to wit, repentance, and the fruits thereof, verse 8. they now desire to be informed further, and more particularly concerning the same, as to particular callings, and duties thereof in order, to escape God's judgment and be saved.

**V. 11. ***{He that hath two coats}* As *Ambrose* says, to those several sorts of men he gave an answer, *singulis conveniens, omnibus unum*; convenient to every particular one, but in substance it was but one precept; that is concerning mercy and charity, which is *plenitudo virtutum*: the fullness of virtue: the most acceptable fruit of repentance, without which, all religion is vain, and the anger of God hangs over men. See 1 Cor. 13. 1. Jam. 1. 26, 27. & 2. 14. 1 John 3. 17. 1 John 4. 20. John 13. 35.

**V. 12. ***{Publicans to be baptized}* These were officers who received tribute, and custom for the Romans. See on Mat. 9. 10. and Mat. 5. 46. this office was erected among the Jews after that Pompey had subdued them (where see *Joseph de bel. Jud.* l. 1. c. 5.) and they became tributary to the Romans, about threescore years before the birth of our Savior: of Publicans Cicero and other Authors make often mention.

**V. 14. ***{Exact no more, &c.}* He taxes the unjust exactions whereby such men commonly grieve the people: therefore chap. 19. 8. Zaccheus in his conversion, proclaims restitution, without which (where there is any ability) can be no true repentance: not that the office was unlawful, but because oppression and religion are inconsistent: therefore, of many sins whereof John Baptist might have admonished them, he instanced only in that to which they were commonly given, and which cannot stand with charity and equity; without which, faith is dead, and all religion counterfeit.

**V. 14. ***{Do violence to no man}* Or *put no man in fear by your violence, and ravages*.

*{your wages}* Let your pay, and allowance of victual, apparel, or appointed quarter content you: extort no more: *If Christian discipline had disallowed all wars, it should have been told these that asked counsel, ut abjicerent arma*, that they ought to lay down their arms. *Augustine Marcellin.*, ep. 5.

**V. 15. ***{People were in expectation}* Or *in suspense*: Considering the unusual austerity and excellency of John, they began to have hope that he was their long expected Messiah. So the Syriac expresses this *προσδοκῶντος*, by the word *סבר* *Sebar*: which signifies hope: as also *סברא* *Sabro*, Acts 24. 15. 1 Tim. 5. 5. etc. also it signifies joyful tidings, the Gospel, etc. Luke 2. 14. Mat. 4. 23. etc.

**V. 16. ***{I indeed baptize you with water &c.}* Meaning that he administered only the external sign and Sacrament, but Christ would give the inward effect thereof, for the washing away their sins. See on Mat. 3. 11. Mark 1. 8. John 1. 26. Acts 1. 5. Acts 11. 16. & 19. 4.

*{and with fire}* Meaning that Christ would cleanse them from their sins, as fire does the gold from dross. See Acts 2. 3.

**V. 17. ***{Whose fan is in his hand}* See on Mat. 3. 12. the preaching of the Gospel is as a fan in Christ's hand: before the truth is preached, all seem one confused heap; chaff and wheat, good and bad are undistinctly one mass in the Church: it is the fan which separates the chaff: thus he showed the Jews, that there was now coming a purging blast of the truth to be preached, which should make them fly out of the Church, as chaff out of the floor, in which they with their many corruptions had hitherto but filled up a place: this separation Christ begins here, which shall be perfected in the life to come; and it is to be understood concerning the effect of the word preached, which proves a *Savour of life unto life* to some, and a Savor of death to death to others: it edifies and comforts some, and enrages others to a heat of persecution of Christ's Ministers; concerning the final and absolute separation to life or death eternal, we may not here pronounce (of that he says Mat. 13. 30. *Let both grow until the harvest, &c.*) That we now see by woeful experience, many separating from us, let none be offended: the wind blows out the chaff from the floor, not the good grain, as *Cyprian. de simpl. praelat. tract.* 3. says let those of light faith, as much as they will, flee away with every breath of temptation *eo purior massa frumenti in horrea Domini reponetur*, said *Tertullian de praescript.*, chap. 3. the more pure heap shall be laid up in the Lord's granary the Church; some of Christ's disciples taking offense at his words (John 6. 66.) separated: do we wonder that some depart from his Church? these things show us to be Christians which we suffer according to the example of Christ; they went out from us, they were not of us, 1 John 2. 19.

**V. 18. ***{Many other things}* This Evangelist writes only the sum of John's preaching, as much as might demonstrate him to be the fore-ordained messenger to prepare the way of Christ, to whose History he addressed his discourse. See Mat. 3. 1. The Spirit of God was not solicitous by the Scriptures to satisfy man's curiosity; but to reveal therein that which is sufficient to make the believer wise to salvation. See Joh. 20. 30, 31. Joh. 21. 25. 2 Tim. 3. 16, 17.

**V. 19. ***{But Herod}* See on Mat. 14. 3. and Mark 6. 17.

**V. 20. ***{Added yet this above all}* This bloody fact helped to fill up the measure of his sin, in that being incensed against the word of God, he revenged himself on the Minister thereof as on an enemy: a sad document to all persecutors of Christ's Ministers, would they understand it.

**V. 21. ***{Jesus also being baptized}* See on Mat. 3. 13. Mark 1. 10. John 1. 32. he would be baptized, *non mundari volens, sed mundare aquas*—says *Ambros. in luc.*, lib. 2. cap. 3. not to be cleansed, but to cleanse the waters, that they being made clean by the flesh of Christ who knew no sin, might have *baptismatis jus*—an ordinance, and right to baptism.

**V. 22. ***{Like a dove}* See on Mat. 3. 16. John 1. 32. Why like a dove? (says *Ambros.*) *simplicitatem enim lavacri requirit gratia.* Our baptism enjoins us to be simple and harmless as doves.

**V. 23. ***{About thirty years of age}* At which age the Priests were to enter into their function, Num. 4. 3, 35. etc.

*{being (as was supposed) the son of Joseph}* For Christ as man, had no father; as God, no mother; as it is said of Melchisedec his type, Heb. 7. 3.

*{Son of Eli}* Luke ascends from the last father to the first, and Matthew contrariwise descends; extending his rehearsal no further than to Abraham; to assure the Jews of God's promise in Christ fulfilled to them. Luke rehearses to Adam, to assure the Gentiles also of their interest in Christ, for as much as they were the sons of Adam, though not of Abraham. Note also that the Jews reckoned their genealogies, not only by the natural, but also by the legal descents: and that whereas divers men had two names: one of the Evangelists reciting one, and the other another, they yet agree in the same person.

**V. 31. ***{son of Nathan}* Zach. 12. 12. 2 Sam. 5. 14. 1 Chron. 3. 5.

**V. 32. ***{son of Booz}* Ruth 2. 1. and 4. 18.

*{Son of Naasson}* 1 Chron. 2. 10.

**V. 34. ***{son of Thara}* Genesis 11. 24. 26.

**V. 36. ***{which was the son of Cainan, which was the son of Arphaxad}* Moses says Arphaxad begat Sala, Genesis 10. 24. but this Evangelist reckons Cainan between Arphaxad and Sala. For the reconciliation of these places, and the better understanding thereof, consider,

1. That all the word of God is infallibly true, and without all contradiction, John 17. 17. 2 Tim. 3. 16. though we understand not some; neither how to reconcile others of them.
2. That not one jot, or tittle of the Scriptures, in matter of substance, has passed away, or ever shall, until all be fulfilled, Mat. 5. 18. Luke 16. 17.
3. That some persons mentioned in holy Scriptures were binomii , such as had two names: so Esau was also called Edom, Gen. 25. 30. Gen. 36. 1, 8. Jerom. adversum Helvid. instances in Raguel Moses' father-in-law, called also Jethro. So also in Gedeon; Ozias, etc. So was Simon also called Peter, John 1. 42. Joseph was also called Barsabas, and surnamed Justus, Acts 1. 23. So Simon was called Niger. So Judas was surnamed Barsabas, Acts 15. 22. Some think Shem was called Melchisedec.
4. That God's Spirit in opening the truth of holy Scriptures (as Lactantius well noted, lib. 2. cap. 9.) would have us know those things which concern life eternal, and the means thereto; other things he omitted or caused to be abridged. See John 20. 30, 31. John 21. 25. therefore Matthew omitted 3. descents, Mat. 1. 8. where it is said, that Joram begat Ozias: Ahaziah, Joash, and Amaziah, are omitted. See 1 Chron. 3. 11, 12. See note on Mat. 1. 8. so Christ is called the Son of David, Mat. 1. 1. you must understand that many generations and forefathers according to the flesh, came between. So Luke 1. 5. Luke 13. 16. etc. It is probable which some say; that seeing Matthew proposed to mark a threefold state of Israel from the promise made to Abraham concerning Christ, unto the time of his coming in the flesh; he reckons three times 14 generations; within which compass holding himself, he was not curious of choice, but indifferently mentions holy and wicked, and so many in succession; his principal scope being to show the truth of God's promise fulfilled in Christ's incarnation. And certainly Moses also intending to show how God ever had his Church, and to declare the truth of his promise concerning Christ, principally took care to show in what line the Church continued; neither reckoning all the Saints which were in that line, nor them only which were holy; nor all descents in a direct line, without intermission of any person.
5.
