**V. 13. ***{pound}* *Mina* here translated a pound, is twelve Ounces and an half: which pound according to five shillings the Ounce, is three pound two shillings and six pence *Sterling money*.

**V. 14. ***{we will not have this man reign over us}* By this he taxes the rebellion of the wicked against Christ; to whose power they shall be unwillingly subject, if they will not willingly be so to his kingdom of grace.

**V. 15. ***{when he was returned}* That is, Christ coming in glory to judge the quick and the dead.

**V. 16. ***{then came the first, saying}* In these several sums and accounts, he describes the several graces and gifts of God of which there must be variable accounts at the day of judgment: the first greatly advanced God's glory; he shall have a proportionable reward: the second endeavored according to his gifts; though far short of the first, he shall have a proportionable reward, and be full of glory and blessedness, though less than the other. See Dan. 12. 3. 1 Cor. 15. 41. The third is the negligent servant, who making no good use of those gifts which he had received, with his vain excuses, is condemned out of his own mouth: and that is taken away which was entrusted to him, as was the Gospel from the Jews, Mat. 21. and given to the Gentiles, as Acts 13. 46. See Luke 8. 18. And the Spirit from *Saul*, 1 Sam. 15. 14. which was bestowed on *David*; and the gifts and Apostleship from *Judas* bestowed on *Matthias*, Acts 1. 26.

**V. 17. ***{well thou good servant}* See on Mat. 25. 21.

**V. 18. ***{the second came}* Mat. 25. 22. See on v. 16.

**V. 19. ***{over five cities}* This is to signify, that the glory of his kingdom in his second coming should be much differing from that which now appears. We now painfully, through scorn, injury, and contempt of the World, negotiate (as it were) for our absent Master; but then we shall be full of glory, joy, and happiness, which Mat. 25. 23. is more plainly expressed, by *enter thou into thy masters joy*.

**V. 20. ***{and another came}* Mat. 25. 24.

**V. 21. ***{I feared thee}* Mat. 25. 25.

**V. 22. ***{out of thine own mouth, &c.}* By the confession of your own conscience, which tells you that you ought not to suppress the gifts of God, and live in idleness.

**V. 23. ***{my money into the bank}* *ἐπὶ* *τὴν* *τράπεζαν*, that is, *τοῖς* *τραπεζίταις*, to the common stock of Bankers, which made profits of moneys by exchange, and lending out for interest.

**V. 24. ***{take from him}* See Mat. 25. 28. and on v. 16.

**V. 25. ***{Lord he hath ten, &c.}* He is rich enough or has grace and glory enough: which we may not conceive Men or Angels shall say in the last judgment, as envying the fullness of glory or blessedness given to any of the Elect, but as witnessing and admiring the abundant Liberality of God.

**V. 26. ***{to every one that hath}* Mat. 13. 12. and 25. 29. Mark 4. 25. Luke 8. 18.

*{from him that hath not, &c.}* The meaning of all is, he that faithfully uses the graces of God, shall have them increased: but even the common graces shall be taken away from him that uses them not to God's glory. Some understand here want of charity making all gifts and excellencies void: *Quisquis charitatem non habet, etiam dona quae percepisse videbatur amittit, Alcuinus* (after *Gregor.*) *de confess.* whosoever has not charity, loses also those gifts which he seemed to have obtained. See 1 Cor. 13. 1, etc.

**V. 27. ***{and slay them before me}* He alludes to the manner of men, executing rebels: the meaning is, that eternal destruction is prepared for God's enemies, as Mat. 25. 41. this Christ speaks to comfort the persecuted and others who observe that the wicked flourish, and securely despise God's laws; assuring them, that though he suffer them to go on for a time, he will yet once appear a severe revenger of iniquity.

**V. 28. ***{he went before}* Mark 10. 32. Toward Jerusalem, where he knew he should suffer death, as he had often informed his disciples that they might know that he willingly suffered for the redemption of man, for whom *he hath poured out his soul unto death*, Isa. 53. 12.

**V. 29. ***{when he was come nigh unto Bethpage, &c.}* Mat. 21. 1. Mark 11. 1. Here is described his last going up to *Jerusalem*. *Bethpage* was a little Village of the Priests at the foot of the East-part of the Mount of *Olives*. See on Mat. 21. 1. of *Bethany*. See John 11. 1.

**V. 30. ***{at your entering ye shall find, &c.}* Mat. 21. 5. Christ being omniscient God, as well as truly Man, saw the absent as if they had been present. See John 1. 48. all this was done that that might be fulfilled which was spoken by the Prophet *Zachary*, 9. 9.

**V. 31. ***{if any ask you}* Christ prevents such difficulties as might in the execution of his command have troubled his Disciples; though he were Lord of all, yet would he not destroy the civil rights of men, by taking away anything without the owner's consent. See Mat. 21. 3.

**V. 32. ***{and they that were sent, &c.}* Mat. 21. 6. Mark 11. 4.

**V. 33. ***{the owners thereof}* Mark 11. 5. They did but rationally require an account of their own goods until they knew, what they were that loosed the colt, and that Jesus had sent them so to do; then they consented, and we read of no more they said. See Mark 11. *and they let them go*.

**V. 34. ***{the Lord hath need of him}* Mark 11. 6.

**V. 35. ***{and they brought him to Jesus}* Matthew 21. 7. Mark 11. 7.

**V. 36. ***{they spread their clothes in the way}* That is, they who were come to the feast, John 12. 12. this was a token of honor done unto him, as to their King, possibly they supposed that he now went up to *Jerusalem* to take the kingdom upon him, which might cause this their extraordinary rejoicing and acclamations, verse 37, 38. See John 12. 13.

**V. 37. ***{at the descent of the mount}* Where he was in sight of *Jerusalem*, unto which he was then going.

*{the disciples began}* That is, the multitude of those which followed him either to hear his doctrine, or see his miracles.

*{for all the mighty works}* Among the rest for the raising *Lazarus* from the dead. See John 12. 17.

**V. 38. ***{blessed be the King}* Psal. 118. 26. See on Mat. 21. 9. Mark 11. 9.

*{peace in heaven}* That God may be reconciled unto us by the *Messias*: that all glory may be given to him for his mercy showed us in these works of Christ: and that the Angels, formerly our enemies for our sin, may become friends and ministers of our safety. See Colos. 1. 20.

**V. 39. ***{Master, rebuke thy disciples}* Mat. 21. 15. That this acclamation ceased not in the way, but came into the very Temple, the children there crying *Hosanna*, it stung the hearts of envious *Pharisees*, so that they came to Christ to silence them.

**V. 40. ***{the stones would immediately cry out}* Meaning that they labored in vain to suppress his testimony; for God would rather cause the senseless creatures to testify for him (as in the rending of the vail of the Temple, in his passion, and of the Rocks, etc. Mat. 27. 51.) than that his will should not be done by him: and indeed Christ's person, doctrine, and miracles, was so evident, that they could not be concealed.

**V. 41. ***{he beheld the city and wept over it}* In his love to the people of *Israel*, zeal of their salvation, and knowledge of their final destruction at hand; in grief and pity he burst out into tears: *plangit eos qui nesciunt cur plangantur, Gregor. hom.* 39. *in Evang.* he bewailed them who knew not why they should be bewailed, who rejoiced when they did evil: all this shows that he took on him, as our nature, so all our affections without sin: so oft-times the Deity (as it were) rested and clouded itself under the vail of his humanity, when the accomplishment of the work of our redemption required his passion, or compassion. See Heb. 4. 15. He was the Minister of the circumcision, Rom. 15. 8. sent to the lost sheep of the house of Israel, Mat. 15. 24. Seeing therefore how willfully they perished, for whose salvation principally he was sent; and that the elect city which God had chosen to put his Name there, rejected their Savior, out of a fatherly and deep compassion, he wept for them who were not sensible of their own misery.

**V. 42. ***{if thou hadst known}* It is a *pathetical* speech, and therefore abrupt: abundance of affectionate thoughts, as it were thronging the heart, like some violent press of people at a door, hinder each other's passage: the sentence may be supplied with *would God*, or how *happy were it for thee if*, etc. The sum is, I do now present the fulfilling of the promise for your salvation; but your destruction is near, because you will not receive it, nor understand that which is so plainly taught you: thus has your pride, who say you are wise and holy, blinded you. *Extremae dementiae est, imprudentiam suam non sentire, & inique agentes, non agnoscere peccatum*, it is extreme dotage not to be sensible of one's own folly, and sinning, not to acknowledge our iniquity. (*Theodoret in Jer.* 8.) and to be unwilling to learn: for as *Chrysostom de reprehens. ferend.* says, it is no fault to be ignorant, but to be unwilling to learn.

*{the things which belong unto thy peace}* Which you now hear spoken, and see done by me; all but for your malice and obstinacy blinding you should bring you to believe, to be justified, and to have peace with God.

*{but now are they hid from thine eyes}* Through the hardness of your heart and incredulity. See Isa. 6. 9. Mat. 13. 14, 15. John 12. 4. Jer. 5. 3. when the measures of a people's iniquity are full, and they shall perish, *providentia ab eo tollitur, ne periturus evadat. Salvian. de gub.* l. 6. providence is taken from them, lest they which must perish, should escape.

**V. 43. ***{Thine enemies shall cast a trench}* Which was done by *Vespasian* the *Roman*: (as it was foretold by *Daniel* c. 9.) and the city and temple demolished, by his son *Titus*.

**V. 44. ***{one stone upon another}* Mat. 24. 2. Mark 13. 2. Luke 21. 6.

*{of thy visitation}* As if he said, in your visitation, in which God manifested himself in my person, offering you mercy: see Gen. 50. 24, 25. Exod. 3. 16. chap. 1. 68.

**V. 45. ***{he went into the temple}* Mat. 21. 12. Mark 11. 15. this was not his first going into the temple mentioned, John 2. 14. but another after that.

**V. 46. ***{it is written}* Isa. 56. 7. See Mark 11. 17.

*{my house is the house of prayer}* See chap. 18. 10. Jer. 7. 11. Matth. 21. 13.

**V. 47. ***{taught daily}* Or *in the day time*: every day after his solemn entrance into Jerusalem, unto the day of his passion: openly contemning the incurable malice of their Priests and Scribes; he taught all day, and at night went out of the city.

**V. 48. ***{Were very attentive}* *ἐξεκρέματο*, that which the Latins expressed by *pendebat ab ore ejus*, they depended on his mouth or attentively hearkened unto him, as taken with astonishment at his doctrine. See Mat. 7. 28, 29. John 7. 46. the *Syriac* gives it, all the people *appensus erat ei, ut audiret eum*, where, *all*, is put for a great or *major part* of them that heard.

## CHAPTER 20

**Verse 1. ***{On one of those days}* Which were between his last coming into Jerusalem, and his death. See Mat. 21. 23. Mark 11. 27.

*{preaching the gospel}* The gladsome tidings of man's salvation, then at hand and near the accomplishment in his death and passion.

*{and the Scribes}* *γραμματεῖς*: *Suidas* interprets this by *νοτάριος*: a Notary, whose office was to write, and recite the written public acts: but in the New Testament, they were *interpreters* of the Law also: There were of those two sorts. 1. *Laics* which were called *γραμματεῖς* *τοῦ* *λαοῦ*: Scribes of the people, Mat. 2. 4. public Notaries in drawing up Deeds, Contracts, and such like secular writings. To these Psal. 45. 1. alludes, *My tongue is the pen* (*סופר* *מהיר* *sopher mabir*. 70. *γραμματέως* *ὀξυγράφου*) *of a swift writer*: out of these possibly the Kings chose some to be Secretaries of State: such were called *γραμματεῖς* *τοῦ* *βασιλέως*, 2 Kings 12. 10. *ספר* *המלך* *sopher hammelech*. So, 2 Sam. 20. 25. 2. The other sort were *Ecclesiastics*: their office was to transcribe, read, and expound the Law of God, and therefore were called *δευτερωταὶ* *τοῦ* *νόμου*. *Epiphan. adv. haeres.* lib. 1. cap. Interpreters, and Doctors of the Law. So was *Saphan*, 2 Kings 22. 3. *סופר* *בית* *יהוה* *sopher beth Jehova*, a Scribe of the house of God. So was also *Ezra*, Ezra 7. 6. *סופר* *מהיר* *בתורת* *משה*, A ready or a swift Scribe in the Law of *Moses*: These being conversant in Scriptures, were principally Text-men, cleaving to the written word of God, whereas the *Pharisees* were mainly for traditions of the Elders. And it is observable, that the *Scribes* and *Pharisees*, agreeing in one resolution of malicing and calumniating Christ, yet much differed in their grounds. So Mat. 9. 3. the *Scribes* accused him of blasphemy; but verse 11. the *Pharisees* accused him of eating with Publicans and Sinners: So Mark 7. 3, etc. They question him concerning the breach of traditions.

*{came upon him}* Gr. *ἐπέστησαν*: the same word is used c. 2. 9. though there concerning good, but here for evil. The Syriac gives it, *they arose up against him*; see Mat. 21. 23. Mark 11. 27. See note on c. 14. 1.

**V. 2. ***{by what authority}* See Mat. 21. 23. Mark 11. 28. Gr. *ἐν* *ποίᾳ* *ἐξουσίᾳ*, by what power; *ἐν* here signifies by, as also Hebr. 1. 1. *he hath spoken unto us* *ἐν* *υἱῷ*, *by his Son*, because he cast out the buyers and sellers out of the temple, and also taught the people; they not knowing, or acknowledging him to be the *Messias*; and conceiving that the government of the temple, and matters of Religion were in their power and disposing, question him by what authority he did those things. See 1 Kings 22. 24.

**V. 3. ***{I will also ask you, &c.}* See Mat. 21. 24.

**V. 4. ***{the baptism of John, &c.}* By baptism he signifies the doctrine and whole ministry of *John*, bearing witness to Christ.

*{was it from heaven or of men?}* That is, of divine, or human institution? See Acts 5. 38. Christ understanding their intention to be only that they might calumniate, not that they might learn and believe; answers their question by a question, and that very satisfactory, if they would have understood: for seeing *John Baptist* testified concerning Christ, that he was the promised Messias, the Son of God; they ought to have believed *John*, whom they believed sent of God: and in so doing, they should not have doubted Christ's authority to purge his temple, and to preach: withal he taxes their perverse unbelief, who knowing in their consciences that *John's* doctrine was from heaven, and that he baptized in the name of Christ, yet would not believe his testimony concerning Christ. See John 3. 36.

**V. 5. ***{they reasoned with themselves, &c.}* Mat. 21. 25.

**V. 6. ***{but if we say of men}* Mat. 21. 26. They feared to be stoned by the people as blasphemers; but feared not God's judgment on their hypocrisy, who withheld the truth in unrighteousness: dissembling their knowledge, lest their secular gains or reputation should be impeached.

**V. 7. ***{they answered that they could not tell}* Mat. 21. 27. thus does God's wisdom nonplus man's, and drive it into unrecoverable straits and disadvantages, whence it cannot unwind itself. See 1 Cor. 3. 19. here was a miserable refuge to lie against their own consciences, before him who is greater than all hearts, and knows all things, 1 John 3.

**V. 8. ***{Neither tell I you}* You dissemble this because you would not confess your wilful unbelief, and therefore I will not answer you, Mat. 21. 27.

**V. 9. ***{a certain man planted a vineyard}* Mat. 21. 33. Mark 12. 1. Isaiah 5. 1. Jer. 2. 21. which is interpreted, Isaiah 5. 7. to be the Jews: the noble vine which God brought out of Egypt, Psal. 80. 8.

*{forth to husbandmen}* That is, committed it to the care of governors and Priests: It was a long time that they had the managing of his vineyard the Church, even from the people of *Israel's* coming into the land of *Canaan*, unto the coming of Christ in the flesh: or as some understand it, unto the final destruction thereof in the demolishing of the temple and city by *Vespasian*, and *Titus*.

**V. 10. ***{sent a servant}* To require in the name of God, fruits worthy amendment of life. See Mat. 21. 34. By servant, he means the *Prophets*; who, though many persons, were but one in office of declaring God's will for man's salvation, speaking one truth, as being inspired all by one Spirit of God.

*{beat him, and sent him away empty}* Mat. 21. 35. the meaning is, they abused, and persecuted the Prophets. See 2 Chron. 36. 16, etc. and sent them away empty, without the fruits which God expected; which fruits are specified, Isa. 5. 7. Gal. 5. 22, 23. in general, their salvation, the fruits of preaching: otherwise they have not labored in vain, their work and reward being with God, Isa. 49. 4.

**V. 11. ***{he sent again, &c.}* See Mat. 21. 36.

**V. 12. ***{he sent the third}* By this definite number is meant many Prophets, at several times, and in several ages: for as he ever had a Church, so he never left them destitute of competent teachers, though sometimes for the wickedness of the people, their contempt of the word, and abuse of the Prophets, the word of God became precious, as in a famine thereof. See 1 Sam. 3. 1. Amos 8. 11.

**V. 13. ***{it may be they will reverence him}* This shows how God's invincible goodness strives with man's wickedness, their unbelief cannot make void his truth, Rom. 3. 3. God had promised them a Savior, and now he sent him, to confirm and accomplish the promises *made unto the fathers*, Rom. 15. 8. this *ἴσως*, it may be, imports not ignorance of the event, which God foresaw: but as it is usually in parables, he thus accommodates human affections to God, that it might more evidently express their desperate impiety in raging against Christ, whom God sent into the world to save them.

**V. 14. ***{that the inheritance may be ours}* Mat. 21. 37. This is to show the vain and impious hope of Jews crucifying Christ; wherein they thought, that by taking him away, who by his mighty works appeared to be the Son of God, they should keep the holy city, honor of the priesthood, and dominion over the Saints, in their own hands: two things are here remarkable. 1. The blind ambition and avarice of the Priests, who would fain [their] usurped authority and tyranny over men's consciences might still be upheld, to which rather than their desires should fail of effect, they would make way through the precious blood of the Lord of life. 2. The admirable goodness of God which could not be defeated by so matchless wickedness of men; all which he well foresaw: but in man's greatest malice, he made his love most clearly shine, turning it to his glory in our redemption and salvation.

**V. 15. ***{they cast him out of the vineyard}* Thus did they, when apprehending Christ, they delivered him as a blasphemer and excommunicate person to the power of the Gentiles to be crucified. See Chap. 23. 1, 2, 21, 23. John 9. 22, 28.

*{what therefore shall the Lord of the vineyard do to them?}* Mat. 21. 40. It is a kind of appeal to their own consciences; as if he had said, Make the case your own, and tell me, what would you do to such tenants? See the like, Isa. 5. 3, etc. God's judgments are so clear, that the sinner's own conscience shall acknowledge their equity, in their own self-condemnation.

*{God forbid}* Mat. 21. 41. Christ first extorted this confession from themselves: they said unto him, *he will miserably destroy those wicked men*, etc. which they said when at first they thought some others, not themselves concerned in this parable: but perceiving that themselves were the wicked men herein personated, they retract their sentence, and say to Jesus, pronouncing the sentence from their own mouth (which this Evangelist, and *Mark* 12. 9. record) *μὴ* *γένοιτο*, let it not be, God forbid: the Syriac gives it, this shall not be. We shall not kill the Son of God, etc.

**V. 17. ***{the stone which the builders, &c.}* Psal. 118. 22. Isa. 28. 16. Acts 4. 11. Rom. 9. 33. 1 Pet. 2. 7. Mat. 21. 42.

*{head of the corner}* Mat. 21. 42. The Syr. interpreter has *praecipuum cornu anguli*, *קרנא* here used as the Hebrew *קרן* *keren*, among other significations, imports a high and eminent place, strength, power, etc. So Christ unites two walls together, meeting in one angle of the corner stone, by which is signified, that Christ is the most high and powerful head of the Church of Jews and Gentiles to be united into one. See John 10. 16. Ephes. 2. 15.

**V. 18. ***{shall fall upon that stone}* They to whom he shall prove a rock of offense through their unbelief, 1 Cor. 1. 23. and thereby also provoke Christ to fall upon them, as a severe Judge to their utter destruction.

**V. 19. ***{against them}* Greek, *πρὸς* *αὐτοὺς*: the Syriac gives it, *concerning them*: the word is often used as here, for *against*: as they say, *πρὸς* *φάρμακα* *θανάσιμα*, *against* deadly poison; so was this monition of Christ to them, wholesome *against* their self-destructive malice; had they not (by despising the counsel of God *against* themselves) made that to be *against* themselves, which otherwise would have been for them, because it was *against* their sin, which had infected and poisoned their souls.

**V. 20. ***{sent forth spies}* Mat. 22. 16. Mark 12. 13. they are named Pharisees and Herodians, a sect instituted by *Herod* the great, who among other errors wherewith they mixed and corrupted the truth of religion, took *Herod* to be the Messias: Of which opinion, though it have many grave Authors, yet others justly doubt: let those who will be further satisfied herein, read *D. Godwin, Moses & Aaron*, lib. 1. cap. 13. *Beza on Matth.* 22. 16. *Isaac Causabon. Exercit.* 1. Num. 5. *ad apparat. Annal. Joh. Drus. praeterit.* lib. 1. Matth. 22. 16. *& ad voc. N. T. Herodiani: Hieronym. on Matth.* 22. 16. *Epiphan. haeres.* lib. 1. cap. 20. *Tertul. de praescript.* cap. 45.

*{which should feign, &c.}* Vainly thinking thereby to have deceived Christ, who knows the secrets of all hearts.

**V. 21. ***{Master we know}* Mat. 22. 16. Mark 12. 14.

*{truly}* Or *of a truth*.

**V. 22. ***{is it lawful for us}* For us who are the peculiar people of God, who gave us this land according to his promise: thus they and the Herodians (who, as *Hierom* on Mat. 22. 16. says, stood mainly for payment of tribute to be made to *Caesar*) would have found something in Christ's answer, whereof they might accuse him to the Romans, to whom they were now become tributary.

**V. 23. ***{he perceived, &c.}* See Mat. 22. 18.

**V. 24. ***{a penny}* Mat. 22. 19. and 18. 28.

**V. 25. ***{Render therefore, &c.}* God commanded to give every man his due, tribute to whom tribute, custom to whom custom is due, Rom. 13. 7. they are deceived who think stealing of customs due to Princes or States is no sin, because a penal statute lies thereon; if they be taken, to forfeit all: for in the Levitical Law, it was penal to restore fourfold, for him that was found, or convicted of stealing ox or sheep, etc. Exod. 22. 1, etc. yet such stealing was no less theft then before God, than now; the moral Law saying, *Thou shalt not steal*.

**V. 26. ***{could not take hold on his words, &c.}* Their purpose being to render him odious unto the people, they hoped by this question to have ensnared him, whatever he should have answered; as if he had said, *It is not lawfull*; then they would possibly have cried; Behold a seditious man, who (like that former Galilean) would embark us in rebellions against *Caesar*, and so bring the Roman sword upon us; if he had said, it is lawful: they would have murmured among the people that he was a flatterer of *Caesar* endeavoring to confirm the servitude and oppression of God's people; and in case they could have wrought but an ill opinion of him in the people, they might destroy him without tumult; their chief aim was that they might find some occasion to have given him up to power and authority, to destroy him whom themselves durst not destroy for fear of the people, or the *Romans* who permitted them not the power of judicature in matters capital, or concerning life and death.

**V. 27. ***{came to him certain of the Sadducees}* Mat. 22. 23. Mark 12. 18. The Sadducees, denying the Resurrection, and holding that the soul died, were *off-sets of Dositheus the Samaritan*, who rejected the writings of the Prophets: and these Sadducees received only the five Books of *Moses*: *Hieronym. adv. Luciferian.* and on Mat. 22. *Epiphan.* lib. 1. 14. (who says, they named themselves Sadducees, *ἀπὸ* *δικαιοσύνης*, of righteousness, which they professed above others) See *Tertullian de praescript.* cap. 45. *Joseph. Antiq.* lib. 18. cap. 2. *Jo. Drusius ad voc. N. T. tom.* 1. *Godwin. Moses & Aaron.* lib. 1. cap. 11. The Sadducees held some things with the Samaritans, though they were Jews: here we may note, that they derived a vain scruple out of *Moses'* law, that they might tempt Christ: but he knowing that they held none of the Prophets as Canonical, but only *Moses*, produced no testimony to confute them, but only out of *Moses* whom they received. *Hierom in Matth.* 22. See v. 37.

**V. 28. ***{Moses wrote}* Deut. 25. 5.

*{that his brother}* Or *next kinsman*, if he were a single man; for that law *ad consanguineos & affines extensa fuit, Shindl. Penteglot.* *יבם*.

**V. 29. ***{Seven brethren}* Or *kinsmen*. See Mat. 22. 25.

**V. 33. ***{Whose wife of them is she}* One absurdity by them received, begat them many: they believed no resurrection: thinking, that if they should grant a resurrection, they must hold also that it must be to an estate like unto this present; subject to infirmities and necessities: whereas *Theophylact* says well, marriage is *βοήθημα* *τῶ* *θνητῷ*, an aid of the mortal, and supplement of that which perishes in time: where then all are eternal, what needs marriage? also they possibly thought that if they should have confessed that there were any angels or spirits, subsisting without a body; then they must also have confessed, that separated souls might live: therefore they denied all: that is, the being of angels and spirits, and the resurrection of the body, and immortality of the soul, which the sober Heathens believed. See Mat. 22. 29. Christ shows them the cause of their gross errors, ignorance of the Scriptures, and power of God to raise the dead.

**V. 34. ***{children of this world}* Men and women here living in this present world: for we must not understand, *children of this world*, here as chap. 16. 8. for men addicted and given over to the care and love of this world, but for all that live in this world: for the children of light do marry as well as others: the sense is, that there shall be a great difference between this present life subject to many necessities, and that which is to come, subject to none: there shall be no more increase of God's Kingdom, the number of the Elect being accomplished; there all secular relations, and interests shall cease, and be no more.

**V. 35. ***{shall be accounted worthy}* He speaks principally of the elect, in whom he here instances. The wicked indeed shall rise again, but to eternal misery (Dan. 12. 2. Joh. 5. 29.) shame and condemnation, which is not worthy the name of life; and therefore as the Scripture calls an impious life, death, 1 Tim. 5. 6. So styles it the unhappy life of the damned, raised from the dead, *the second death*, Rev. 20. 6.

**V. 36. ***{neither can they die any more}* Therefore shall there be no more matrimony for the preservation of mankind.

*{children of God}* By adoption in Christ, with whom they are co-heirs of glory and happiness, so complete, that they want nothing. See Rom. 8. 23. 1 John 3. 2.

*{children of the resurrection}* This is a phrase familiar in Scriptures; *children of youth*, Psalm 127. 4. *children of Zion*, Psalm 129. 2. *son of the bow*, that is, *the arrow*, Job 41. 28. *sons of strength*, that is, *strong men*, 2 Sam. 2. 7. 1 Kings 1. 52. Deut. 3. 18. so they said, *בן־מות* *ben maveth*, *a son of death*, 1 Sam. 20. 31. worthy of death, or designed to death, Psalm 102. 21. so Acts 3. 25. *υἱοὶ* *τῶν* *προφητῶν*, etc. sons of the Prophets: so they said, *בן־שנה* *ben shana*, *the son of a year*, 1 Sam. 13. 1. for that which was of one year's continuance or age: *the son of an hundred years*, Gen. 17. 17. *the son of a night*, Jonah 4. 10. *son of the morning*, Isa. 14. 13. *sons of God*, Job 38. 7. that is, the Angels, etc.

**V. 37. ***{Moses shewed}* Exod. 3. 6. See on verse 27. See Mat. 22. 32, etc. Mark 12. 26.

**V. 38. ***{God of the dead}* Of them that live not at all: for the souls of the Elect live with him in joy and blessedness, even while their bodies rest in the dust of the earth, until the last day.

**V. 39. ***{thou hast well said}* Because he confuted their adversaries the Sadducees.

**V. 40. ***{they durst not ask him}* That is, the Sadducees were so confuted, that they durst not ask him any more captious questions; the Pharisees it seems did, Mat. 22. 35, 36.

**V. 41. ***{how say they, &c.}* Mat. 22. 42. Mark 12. 35.

**V. 42. ***{the Lord said}* Psalm 110. 1.

**V. 44. ***{How is he then his Son?}* Christ, as eternal God, was *David's* Lord; and as man, his son, that is, of his lineage, Rom. 1. 3.

**V. 45. ***{in the audience of all, &c.}* Hypocrisy being, as it were the canker at the root of Religion, eating up the life and power thereof: It concerned his disciples and all men, to beware of it: therefore he here did, that which his spirit enjoins by Saint *Paul*, 1 Tim. 5. 20. *Those that sin rebuke before all men, that others also may fear*.

**V. 46. ***{beware of the Scribes}* Mat. 23. 5. Mark 12. 38. Luke 11. 43.

*{which desire to walk in long robes}* He condemns not the use of any civil and convenient garments, but the excess and abuse thereof. The Eastern people did use, and still do use long robes: and the Prophets had a kind of mantle, or proper garment distinguishing them from other men; and it was meet for the teachers so to be clothed, that in their habit there might appear more than vulgar gravity: but the Scribes (as some since) abused this to pride and ostentation: placing Religion chiefly in the outward appearance, wherewith they veiled their detestable impiety.

**V. 47. ***{which devour widows houses}* Mat. 23. 14. who under pretense of holiness, long prayers, and other religious exercises, consume their estates who entertain them: Or by deceiving the trust which friendless widows repose in them, whom they took to be so religious. See 1 Tim. 3. 5, 6. Mark 12. 40.

*{greater damnation}* That is, an heavier weight of God's judgment; because the sinning under shows of godliness, do more cause the holy Name of God to be blasphemed of the wicked (who usually cast all personal sins, upon the very religion professed by those sinners) than they do (who do not so much as pretend to sanctity) when they sin.

## CHAPTER 21

**Verse 1. ***{And saw the rich men}* Mark 12. 41.

*{the treasury}* See on verse 4. there were other treasuries about the temple; but this was a chest set at the Eastern door, through which all the people passed in and out, that every man with his own hand, might cast in that, which either was taxed upon him, or he voluntarily offered, or gave unto the poor. See 2 Kings 12. 9. 2 Chron. 24. 8. Nehem. 10. 38.

**V. 2. ***{two mites}* Mark 12. 42. the least money they had in use among them, which they called *χαλκὸς*, Syr. *octavus*, *octaves*, or eight part of that which they called *ὀβόλος*. *Theophylact*. See hereof *Beza major. annot. in Marc.* 12. 42.

**V. 3. ***{more than they all}* More in proportion, considering her estate and theirs: and because God esteems not the gift by man's value thereof, but by the giver's heart.

**V. 4. ***{The offerings of God}* That is, those things which as freewill offerings were consecrated to God, for the use or service of the temple, and his public worship: we may take this *εἰς* *τὰ* *δῶρα*, for *ἐν* *τοῖς* *δώροις*, among the gifts: or the treasury and continent, for the treasure contained therein: that is, their *ארון* *aron*, mentioned 2 Kings 1. 10. (as the Chaldees called it, and the name was now in use, *קרבן* *Corban*, which *Josephus lib.* 4. *c.* 4. *Antiq.* interprets, *the gift of God*) so is it interpreted, Mark 7. 11. *κορβᾶν* (*ὅ* *ἐστι* *δῶρον*) and Matt. 15. 5. where the Greek has *δῶρον*, the Syriac has *קורבני* *curbanni, my gift*, or oblation, of the Hebrew *קרב* *kerab*: *Josephus*, also *lib.* 1. *adv. Ap.* calls it *δῶρον* *Θεῷ*. So here verse 5. such gifts are called *ἀναθήματα*, gifts consecrated to God and holy uses: yet by a synecdoche, this also imported their treasury in which they kept the gifts offered to God, Matt. 27. 6. *it is not lawfull to cast it* *εἰς* *τὸν* *κορβανᾶν* which there the Syriac calls *בית* *קורבנא* *beth Curbono*, the house of oblation: for which Mark 12. 41, 43. is set, *γαζοφυλάκιον*, the treasury of the temple: so *Suidas* says, *Κορβονᾶς*, *παρὰ* *Ἰουδαίοις* *ἱερὸς* *θησαυρὸς*: the *Corban* with the Jews was the sacred treasury.

**V. 5. ***{and as some spake}* Matt. 24. 1. Mark 13. 1. As some of the disciples spoke in admiration of that beautiful structure.

*{and gifts}* As plate, jewels, or the like precious things dedicated to the use or ornament of the temple, by the devotion of them who came thither to worship: see on verse 4. the word here used, *ἀνάθημα* or *ἀνάθεμα*, signifies that which is devoted, or consecrated to God, and it is taken either in the good or evil sense; it comes of *ἀνατιθέναι* which signifies to hang by or separate from common use, as trophies of victories, things dedicated for deliverances or other monuments of thankfulness, used oftentimes to be by the walls, or on the pillars of temples; but here the conjecture of *Theophylact* is very probable, I think (says he) that they speak *de sculptis & tornatis ut erant Palmæ & Cherubim & Similia*, of the engraving and polishing, as were in the palms and cherubims or the like (carved in the beautiful stones of the temple) *ταῦτα* *γὰρ* *ἴσως* *τὰ* *ἀναθήματα*, *φησὶν*—for perhaps he calls these gifts.

**V. 6. ***{which ye behold}* Which you so much regard and admire: see Matt. 24. 2. Mark 13. 4.

*{one stone upon another}* Chap. 19. 43. God would have Jerusalem the place which he had chosen to put his name there, and to which he had for a time restrained the public service, and sacrificing (prefiguring Christ the Savior to come and suffer there) utterly destroyed, with the temple, in which the Jews so much gloried; after that Christ was come, and had suffered there, and had accomplished the work of our redemption: to give the Jews to know, that now all their ceremonies were ended, he being come and having finished all things which they prefigured; and that they ought no more to stick in the now dead and antiquated letter of that Levitical law; circumcision, sacrifices, etc. but to come to Christ the end of the law to every believer. And because God well knew, how unwillingly they would part from their rites, in which they had been instructed hitherto; and wherein they esteemed themselves more excellent and holy than any nation in the world, to join in the faith of Christ, and therein to be equaled with the Gentiles whom they despised; therefore God (who as *Chrysostome adver. Jud. orat.* 1. says) everywhere present, and filling every place, yet reduced *τὴν* *μὲν* *λατρείαν*, *εἰς* *θυσίας* *τὰς* *δὲ* *θυσίας* *εἰς* *τόπον*, *τὸν* *δὲ* *τόπον* *εἰς* *καιρὸν*, *τὸν* *δὲ* *καιρὸν* *εἰς* *μίαν* *πόλιν*: his worship to sacrifices, sacrifices to a certain manner, that manner to a certain time, and that time to one city; and again overthrew that city, to declare the utter determination and abolition of that law and ceremonies, which were tied to that place, and which were nowhere in the whole world to be used but there.

**V. 7. ***{When shall these things be?}* Mat. 24. 3: by what sign or signs shall we know the time when these sad things shall come upon us?

**V. 8. ***{Take heed that ye be not deceived}* He answered not directly to their question, but that which was more necessity. Bids them look to themselves, that they be not seduced. Mat. 14. 4, 5. Mark 13. 5, 6.

*{the time draweth near}* That seducers shall go out into the world. See 1 John 2. 18.

*{go ye not therefore after them}* That is, adhere not to them: believe them not, neither hearken to their doctrine, lest you cast yourselves into temptation, and God give you over to strong delusions, to believe lies, and perish. See Deut. 13. 3. 8. Jer. 23. 16, 17. Jer. 27. 9. Mat. 24. 5, 11, 23, 24, 26.

**V. 9. ***{When ye shall hear of wars}* Mat. 24. 6. When the Jews had preferred the murderous *Barabbas*, (John 18. 40. Acts 3. 14.) before the Lord of life and peace; divine justice followed them, continually; so that they were almost never free from seditious tumults, insurrections, and wars, until they had brought (as themselves had imprecated) the blood of Jesus upon their own, and children's heads in the final destruction of their temple, and city.

**V. 10. ***{Nation against nation}* Mat. 24. 7.

**V. 11. ***{earthquakes}* Mat. 24. 7.

**V. 12. ***{They shall lay}* Mat. 24. 9. Mark 13. 6. Before these things, the Jews and others shall lay violent hands on you, persecute and imprison you: that they may fill up their measure of sin, and provoke the heavy wrath of God to fall upon them.

*{for my Names sake}* For the profession of your faith in me.

**V. 13. ***{for a testimony}* When you shall certainly know the truth of that which I tell you beforehand, that you may thence be assured of the victory, and eternal reward; as also of the destruction of your enemies, Philip. 1. 28. 2 Thess. 1. 5.

**V. 14. ***{settle it therefore in your hearts}* Mat. 10. 19. Mark 13. 11. Keep that fixed in your hearts and memories; which I have promised you (that is to assist you, as you have need, with extraordinary wisdom, to answer) that when you have experience thereof, it may comfort you, in assurance of my assistance in all other trials.

**V. 15. ***{For I will give you a mouth}* See Acts 6. 10. I will give you wisdom, and readiness of speech, to answer for yourselves appositely and prudently, boldness and courage, which all your enemies shall admire but not be able to resist. See Acts 4. 8, 13. See Exod. 4. 10, 11, 12, etc.

*{shall not be able to gain-say}* Which is not so to be understood as if their enemies' impudency and spirit of contradiction, should not break out against them; but because the truth which they should speak should be so evident, that it should have manifest victory over their enemies' falsehood, who will never want endeavor, but ever shall want success. See Acts 4. 13. Acts 6. 10. Acts 7. 48. Acts 26. 24. etc.

**V. 16. ***{by your parents}* Mat. 10. 21. Such a cursed influence has Satan on persecutors, that he causes them to violate and break the strongest bands of nature, and to precipitate parents against their own children, brother against brother.

**V. 17. ***{hated of all}* Mat. 10. 22. Subversion of kingdoms begins with persecution of good men. Here, all, is put for very many, or the greatest part, in respect whereof the rest seem as nothing, no considerable party, or such as dare show themselves: such a sad appearance of a church was there in Israel, when Elijah cried—*I only am left, and they seek my life to take it away*—1 Kings 19. 14.

**V. 18. ***{There shall not an hair}* Mat. 10. 30. 1 Sam. 14. 45. 2 Sam. 14. 11. Acts 27. 34. He expresses this great care and providence over his, that we knowing how God loves us, may love him again, and cleave to him by faith of a pure heart; *cui ad beatitudinem fides est via, &c. Fulgent. de incarn, &c.* whose way to blessedness is faith.

**V. 19. ***{in your patience possess}* Live joyfully even under the cross, assured of your peace with God; who will not suffer you to be tried above that he will make you able. Rom. 5. 3. 1 Thess. 1. 6. 2 Cor. 1. 5. 1 Cor. 10. 13. It is patience says *Cyprian de bono pat. quæ nos Deo & commendat & servat*, which both commends to, and keeps us in God: which tempers anger, bridles the tongue, governs the mind, and preserves peace, etc. It is, says *Gregor. hom.* 35. *radix omnium custosque virtutum*, the root and keeper of all virtue: anger is a short madness, *μέγα* *θηρίον*, a great and dangerous beast, says *Theophylact in Luc.* 18. neither is he to be esteemed of less fortitude, *qui violentam & in semetipso inclusam feram superat iracundiam, &c.* who overcomes that violent and upshut beast in himself, anger, than he that conquers a lion: *vid. Lactant. l.* 1. *c.* 9. The same defines patience, *malorum quæ aut inferuntur, aut accidunt, cum æquanimitate perlatio. l.* 1. *c.* 23. Patience is a bearing of those injuries which are offered, or do happen, with a quiet moderation of mind: we must therefore take heed, says *August. ep.* 5. *ad Marcellin*: that we lose not our patience, which is of more worth (or is to be esteemed of more value) *quam omne quod potest etiam invito inimicus auferre*, than all that which an enemy can take away from a man against his will.

**V. 20. ***{Jerusalem compassed}* Matthew 24. 15. Mark 13. 14.

**V. 21. ***{Then let them which are}* As soon as they perceive the approaches of the enemy to beleaguer them, let them flee.

*{to the mountains}* Judea was bounded *northward* with *Libanus* or *Libanon*, that high and famous hill of *Phœnice*: on the south with the mountains of *Idumæa*, and *Arabia Petræa*. See *Plin. l.* 6. *c.* 28. and *Hieron. de Loc. Act.* A. Now as he foretells their dangers not to terrify but admonish them for their better security against the common enemy, and calamity; so does he point out the safest places for them to retreat to; and so the church of the faithful then at Jerusalem, were admonished by a divine revelation, a voice crying out to them from the inward parts of the temple, *transmigremus ex his sedibus*: let us go hence: *Ierom. l.* 2. *ep.* 8. out of *Iosephus*; and they went thence to *Pella*, a city of Manasseh beyond Jordan; the rest perished in great multitudes. See *Ioseph de bel. Iud. l.* 1. 17.

*{in the midst of it}* In the midst of it, that is Jerusalem or Judea.

**V. 22. ***{for these be the days of vengeance}* Which he says, to show them that nothing of all this should come by chance, but by the divine providence of God, thus punishing the Jews' obstinacy, who would take no warnings, but would needs contrary to this admonition, flock to Jerusalem from other nations (where they lived more secure) at the feast of Passover, where they were shut up, with the rest to destruction, by sword, pestilence and famine, *Joseph. q. s. l.* 7. *c.* 17.

**V. 23. ***{in those days}* When Jerusalem shall be besieged by the Romans. See *Joseph. de bel. Iud. l.* 7. *c.* 12.

**V. 24. ***{until the times}* In which God will permit the Gentiles to oppress the Jews: and until the fullness of the Gentiles be called to Christ and come in: before the recalling of the Jews, Rom. 11. 25, 26. Dan. 9. 27.

**V. 25. ***{signs in the Sun}* Matt. 24. 29. Mark 13. 24. 2 Peter 3. 10. 12.

**V. 26. ***{hearts failing them}* *ἀποψυχόντων* ready to breathe out their souls: so *Suidas* out of *Thucydides l.* 1. and so *Sophocles Ajac.* says *ἀποψύχειν* *βίον*; to breathe out life: the Syriac interpreter, *tremor extrudens animas*.

*{powers of Heaven}* The very fabric of the heavens shall be shaken, and the whole world melt with heat, 2 Pet. 3. 10. this he speaks of the last day.

**V. 27. ***{coming in a cloud}* Matt. 24. 30. Acts 1. 9. 11. Rev. 1. 7. So shall they see him in his majesty, whom they would not hear in his humility. *Gregor. hom.* 1. *in Evang.*

**V. 28. ***{then look up}* This is spoken for the faithful that shall remain to the coming of Christ; that they should not then be discouraged, but look up for the promise of Christ, eternal redemption, from all oppression and evil. See Romans 8. 22, 23. Those who with good hope expect something at hand, use to look up.

*{lift up your head}* *Gregor. q. s.* interprets it by *exhilarate corda*, cheer up your hearts: lift up your minds unto the joys of your heavenly country; then rejoice, because now you shall presently find, and be eternally united to your redeemer.

**V. 29. ***{Behold the fig-tree}* Matt. 24. 32. Mark 13. 28.

**V. 31. ***{When ye see these things}* Matt. 24. 33.

**V. 32. ***{shall not pass away}* Matt. 24. 34. For those things which he spoke concerning the destruction of Jerusalem, came to pass about forty years after.

**V. 33. ***{Heaven and earth shall pass}* Matt. 5. 18. Matt. 24. 35. Mark 13. 31.

**V. 34. ***{Take heed to yourselves}* He exhorts them to vigilance by the certainty of dangers to come, and the uncertainty of the time when they shall come: now because surfeiting, drunkenness and cares, do commonly so oppress the drowsy soul, that it cannot look up to the things which concern the life to come; he admonishes them to beware of these. *Heraclitus* said well, *ξηρὴ* *ψυχὴ* *σοφὴ*, the dry soul is wise: *Synes. de insomn.* Drunkenness makes one worse than a fool. See on chap. 1. 15.

**V. 35. ***{as a snare shall it come}* Eccles. 9. 12. As a snare takes the bird or beast, when they think nothing of danger; so shall that day steal upon a secure world; while they are feeding or sporting. See 1 Thess. 5. 3. Therefore our fears should be to us, *sequentis iræ præcones, Greg. hom.* 1.

**V. 36. ***{Pray always}* Pray without giving over: he means not without intermission. See chap. 18.

*{and to stand}* Meaning so to stand in judgment, as they that are justified by Christ: which the secure wicked shall not do. See Psalm 1. 5. Ephes. 6. 13.

**V. 37. ***{in the day time he was teaching}* See chap. 19. 47.

*{abode in the mountain}* To spend the night in prayer. See Matt. 14. 23. Mark 6. 46. Thus joined he preaching and praying, who was sent to instruct and reconcile men unto God; which he the more diligently performed, knowing that his departure from the world, now drew near.

**V. 38. ***{all the people came early}* So chapter 19. 48. All the people, that is, the greater part were attentive to hear him: the diligence and early rising, and attention of this people, in hearing the Gospel, shall rise in judgment against those who lazily come to hear, and as drowsily and carelessly attend unto them.

## CHAPTER 22

**Verse 1. ***{Now the feast of}* Matthew 26. 2. Mark 14. 10. John 13. 2. 27.

*{unleavened bread}* At the Passover yearly to be observed, they were to have no leaven in their habitations, Exod. 12. 7, etc. the moral whereof, see 1 Cor. 5. 7, 8.

*{which is called the Passover}* Gr. *πάσχα*. Syr. *פצחא* *petscho* or *pascha*. Hebr. *פסח* *pesah*. *Jerom* sometimes reads it *Phase*. Some read *φάσιχ* and *πάσχα*. So *Greg. Nazianz. orat.* 42. *Epiphan. adv. hæres. l.* 2. 50. 1, etc. It represented Israel's passage out of Egypt *ἱστορικῶς*, historically, mystically, our coming out of the house of sin's bondage, unto our promised rest. *Gregor. Nazian. q. s.* See Exod. 12. 27. and the *Angels* passing over the *Israelites'* houses marked with the blood of the *Lamb*, and smiting the firstborn of the Egyptians. By a metonymy, it signifies the sacrifice, or *Lamb* to be killed and offered in remembrance of that deliverance, Exod. 12. 21. Also it imports that feast or day on which the *Lamb* was to be killed: the fourteenth day of the first month, 2 Chronicles 35. 1. Exodus 12. 6. Or it signifies the worship or ceremony, action, feasts, and eight days' solemnity appointed by God: to this feast once a year, all the males were to come and appear before the Lord. Exodus 23. 17. in the place which he should choose; Deuteronomy 16. 16. See *Is. Casaubon. Exercit.* 16. *n.* 28. *Ioh. Drusius ad voc. N. T. tom.* 1. *Pascha: Godwin, Moses* and *Aaron. l.* 3. *c.* 4. *c.* 7.

**V. 2. ***{how they might kill him}* The cause of this their solicitous counsel is hereto annexed: they would destroy Christ, but feared the people, lest they should rescue him: or fall on them, who assaulted him, who did so many admirable works of power and mercy among them: therefore they consulted what probable fault they might pretend against him: so that although they would take him away, more like thieves and murderers, than judges; yet would they have some show of justice to color their wickedness.

**V. 3. ***{Then entered Satan}* Matthew 26. 14. God taking from him his common graces, the Devil took possession of him. (See 1 Samuel 16. 14. Matthew 12. 45.) The Evangelist shows how the priests obtained their desire against Christ: Satan tempted *Judas*, to serve their malice; by observing *Judas'* covetous mind, he suggested hope of gain, if he would betray his Master. See John 13. 2.

*{surnamed Iscariot}* He names him thus, to difference him from others of that name, *Judas*. See John 14. 2. See on John 6. 71.

**V. 4. ***{He went his way, &c.}* See on Matt. 26. 14.

*{and captains}* Or *rulers of the temple*. See Acts 4. 1. These had the charge of guarding the temple, verse 52.

**V. 5. ***{and they were glad}* So sweet is revenge where the Devil rules: so welcome is anything to such, which complies with their own mischievous desires and designs: probably they thought it some advantage to their cause, that one of Christ's own disciples and domestics, appeared on their party against him: therefore, however they might think of the traitor, as *Augustus Cæsar*, or *Aurelianus* are said to have done in such cases, yet they were glad of the treason.

**V. 6. ***{in the absence of the multitude}* Or *without tumult*: see note on Matthew 26. 16. *ἄτερ* *ὄχλου* without tumult, in the absence of the multitude: *ὄχλος* signifying either; as comparing this place with Mat. 13. 36. Mat. 21. 8. John 7. 49. Luke 6. 17. Acts 6. 7. will make it appear. The present sense is, when the people which were wont to follow him were away, lest there should be any tumult or uproar about him, which might bring them in danger of the Roman governor's displeasure, or violence of the people, the chief priests had heretofore cautioned, and *Judas* was impiously careful to comply with them herein: therefore he fitted the time when Jesus in the silent night, was out of the city with his disciples in the garden.

**V. 7. ***{then came}* Mat. 26. 17. Mark 14. 12. According to the law; the 14th day of the first month, from that evening that solemnity was to begin: the Syriac gives it, *in which the custome was to kill, &c.*

**V. 8. ***{he sent Peter and John}* Mat. 26. 18. Two disciples most familiar with their Lord: he sent not *Judas*, though he were the steward; Christ well foresaw what he had designed: not a supper, but destruction for Christ.

**V. 10. ***{bearing a pitcher}* Mark 14. 13. *κεράμιον* *ὕδατος*; a pitcher of water, either for purification or some other domestic use. Some give it *lagenam* a bottle, but more improperly; the Syr. word *גרב* here used signifies either; it signifies also *hydream*, a waterpot.

**V. 11. ***{where is the guest-chamber?}* Where we may eat the Passover. See Mark 14. 15.

**V. 12. ***{a large upper room}* See Mark 14. 15: the word *ἀνώγεον* here used, signifies, as *Suidas'* interpreter gives it, *cœnaculum in superiore domus parte situm*: a room in the upper part of a house, where they used to eat: so called in the Greek of its distance from the earth; so the Syriac *עליתא* *halitho* which coming of *עלה* *hala ascendit, &c.* signifies a room into which they ascended by many steps: such rooms they had, as for ordinary entertainment of strangers, so specially for the feast of Passover.

**V. 13. ***{and found it as he had said}* Both the signs and marks told them by Christ, whereby they might know the place, and be assured of the success: all which was extraordinary in respect of the dangerous and active malice of the scribes and Pharisees, against any that should entertain Christ, or so much as confess him. Joh. 9. 22. Also in regard that it was near the feast, that their rooms were generally taken up beforehand, by reason of so great a confluence of people, then coming up from all parts of Judea and other nations: the consideration of all this, gave them to know that his providence ruled all; that they might not be dejected at anything which they should see come to pass contrary to their expectation in his passion.

**V. 14. ***{And when the hour, &c.}* Matt. 26. 20. Mark 14. 17. Which was the evening, Exod. 12. 6.

*{he sate down}* *ἀνέπεσε* *accubuit*, as the Syriac reads; *κοινῷ* *τρόπῳ* after the manner of those countries. See on chapter 7. 36.

**V. 15. ***{with desire have I desired}* *ἐπιθυμίᾳ* *ἐπεθύμησα*: it is a Hebrew manner of speech, to signify his earnest and longing desire of the accomplishment and fulfilling the will of his Father for man's redemption, which he knew should presently follow that Passover.

*{to eat this Passover with you}* He desired before his death to express his matchless love to them, to give them an example of humility, and to ordain the seal of the New Testament, the sacrament of the *Lord's Supper*, the pledge of their spiritual communion with him: and by them to commend it to the church as a remembrance of his passion until his return to judgment: which was then necessary to be done, when the Passover being accomplished in his death, was thence with all the legal ceremonies to be abolished.

**V. 16. ***{until it be fulfilled}* Until that which now is presented in the figure, be really exhibited in the oblation of myself, once for all to take away the sin of the world, John 1. 29. and until my rising again to immortality, the first degree of my exaltation, and spiritual kingdom of glory; of which verse 18. Matth. 26. 29.

**V. 17. ***{he tooke the cup}* This first mentioning the taking of the cup, appertains to the celebration of the Passover, and not unto that which is mentioned, verse 20. This was the cup of thanksgiving, which they used also in other their peace-offerings; wherein they took up the cup of wine in their hands, and blessed God, to which the Apostle alludes, 1 Cor. 10. 16. calling the cup in the *Lord's Supper* used, *The cup of Blessing*: properly, for the *Lord's Supper*, is the *Eucharist*, and *Sacrament of Thanksgiving*: so Christ *tooke the Cup, and gave thankes, &c.* Matt. 26. 27.

**V. 18. ***{I will not drink}* Matth. 26. 29. Mark 14. 25.

**V. 19. ***{and he tooke bread}* Matthew 26. 26. Mark 14. 22. 1 Cor. 11. 23.

*{this is my Body}* That is, the sacramental sign of my body; for instituting a new *Sacrament*, he speaks after a sacramental manner, wherein the things signified are spoken for the sign; as verse 7. the Passover must be killed, that is, the Paschal Lamb, the remembrance or sign of the destroying *Angel's* passing over, and sparing the Israelites, Exod. 12. 12, etc. so he calls the cup, the New Testament, or covenant in his blood; that is, the sign or remembrance of his blood-shedding.

**V. 20. ***{which is shed for you}* Understand this after the manner of the Prophets' speaking, in which, things to come use to be pronounced *sub enunciatione præteriti, Fulgent. object. Ar.* under a declaration of that which is past.

**V. 21. ***{Behold the hand of, &c.}* Matt. 26. 21. Psalm 41. 9. Mark 14. 18. John 13. 21. As if he had said, behold, or consider a horrid and impious thing, although I am about presently to suffer death for you; though I leave you in this now instituted sacrament, the symbols and pledges of my dearest love to mankind (John 3. 16.) for a remembrance of my death, until my coming again to judgment, to be observed in the church; yet the hand which betrays me, is with me at this table; and while he eats with me of my bread, he is meditating how to betray me to death.

*{with me at the Table}* That is, *Judas* who will presently betray me, now at table with me.

**V. 22. ***{was determined}* By God in his eternal counsel, Acts 4. 28. Revel. 13. 8. God determined not sin, but he determined the salvation of man by Christ his being betrayed, and being crucified: so draws he good out of evil; so gave he Christ into the hands and power of sinners, that he satisfying God's justice for us, we might be saved: so God acted that to good, which impious men designed to evil, and acted most sinfully: observe the several ends here proposed. Judas betrayed Christ for gain, the Devil put it into Judas' heart so to do, in hope to take him away who destroyed his works: the priests were instigated hereto by their malice and envy, because their honor and gain were impeached by Christ's preaching and miracles; *Pilate* gave sentence to please the people; the people cried, Crucify him, to ingratiate with their rulers; the soldiers acted as the priests, *Jews and Devils moved them*; but God, out of his unspeakable love and mercy, permitting these voluntary agents every one herein to act as he would, brings all to his determinate will and counsel for man's salvation, which none of these actors either knew, or would: nor is it the least admirable effect of God's providence that some of them who were to be saved by the death of Christ, should be actors in, and promoters and carriers on of the same, which is evident, Acts 2. 36. 37, 38, etc.

**V. 23. ***{to enquire among themselves}* John 13. 22. They looked one upon another, doubting of whom he spoke; probably enquiring among themselves, whether any of their company knew anything concerning any plot against their Master.

**V. 24. ***{there was also strife}* Matt. 20. 24. The occasion of contention about superiority is set down; whether it revived again on this occasion: or that this Evangelist relating the same thing, follows not the same order of the story, it skills not much: the same controversy was on foot at other times among them, Matt. 18. 1. It was a dangerous symptom of a great remainder of the old man in them: see 1 Cor. 3. 3. which ought to be a document of caution and humility unto us; considering men otherwise so excellent, had yet such failings: as also of confidence, that God will not cast us off for our infirmities, who tolerated so much in them, whom he made lights of the world.

**V. 25. ***{he said unto them}* Matt. 20. 25. Mark 10. 42.

*{called benefactors}* Job 12. 21. *εὐεργέται*: the Hebrews called their nobles *נדיבים* *nedibim* liberal, beneficent, Psalm 113. 8. Psalm 47. 10. from *נדב* *nadab, he gave willingly*, or *liberally*. So was *Ptolomie King of Egypt* styled *εὐεργέτης*, from his beneficence to the cities of Greece, which being at first given to princes and great lords, for their personal virtue; at last it became common to princes and lords, as *Augustus*, and other titles, whether in their persons they were such or not: the meaning of Christ here is, that he would not have the ministers of the Gospel affect any high and ambitious titles of domination.

**V. 26. ***{is greatest among you}* In gifts, authority, or other qualities: meaning that such must carry themselves without pride, arrogance, ambition, violence, oppression, and contempt of others; and with that humility and moderation, which would become the meanest in the church: not that he appointed equality of ministers, for he appointed orders in the church, which cannot be without the relations of inferior and superior, only he prohibits tyranny and domineering, Romans 12. 8. 1 Cor. 12. 28. 1 Timothy 5. 17. Hebr. 13. 17, 24. 1 Pet. 5. 3.

**V. 27. ***{Whether is greater}* By another argument he teaches them, that they are not called to rule, and domineer, but to serve: as if he said, I might justly challenge dominion: yet have I taken on me the form of a servant, who am Lord over all; I came to serve, not to be served: how much less ought you to dream of dominion, and worldly greatness?

**V. 28. ***{Ye are they which continued, &c.}* Matt. 19. 28. He declares that they who had shared with him in his suffering, should also communicate with him in glory.

*{in my temptations}* In my persecutions and troubles, Hebr. 2. 18. and 4. 15. James 1. 12. You who constantly adhered to me, neither scandaled at, nor separating from me for my doctrine, as those, John 6. 67. You, who neither for fear or favor of men were ashamed to confess me, as many of the chief rulers did, John 12. 42. You who have been constant to me in all those injuries, persecutions and tribulations, which I have suffered, Matt. 19. expresses this by—*ye that have followed me in the regeneration*, which here is called temptations: indeed God, among mere men, corrects his sons, and exercises their patience by sundry trials and temptations, that being tried, and persevering to do his will in all these trials, they might know that of themselves, which God foreknew concerning their constancy: so tried he *Abraham*, Gen. 22. 1. with that grievous precept; and these temptations must try the whole church, which must through many such enter into the kingdom of God, Acts 14. 22: which began in Christ her head, *who was in all points tempted like as we are, yet without sin*, Heb. 4. 15.

**V. 29. ***{I appoint unto you a Kingdom}* As if he said: your present contention is vain: you are appointed to a heavenly kingdom: only learn patience of me: and be contented to go to that joy (as I shall do) by the way of temporal afflictions: shake off this dream of secular greatness and lift up your minds to eternal blessedness: here we must serve, that we may reign there.

**V. 30. ***{That ye may eat and drink}* Matt. 19. 28. From his last supper, he derives this manner of speaking to signify the effect thereof, life eternal, by their participating of him: this he speaks, *ἐκ* *μεταφορᾶς*—*Theophylact*, by a metaphor taken from those who have the honor to sit at table, with kings; as if he had said, you shall be highly honored.

*{sit on thorns}* Matth. 19. 28. The meaning is, that you may partake with me in glory and happiness: now if this condition remain for you, why do you contend for a miserable and momentary primacy in this world?

**V. 31. ***{Simon Simon}* This ingemination and doubling of his name, is to stir him up to a serious attention, and to him does Christ address his speech, before the rest, though all of them were concerned; first, because he foresaw that *Peter* (being more fervent and confident than the rest, and so more exposed to Satan's charges) would more grievously fall, than his fellow disciples: secondly, because afterward he should excel them also in a singular example of constancy: or as *Chrysostom* (on John 17. 12.) thinks, because he had high thoughts, as trusting to some merit of his more fervent love to Christ.

*{satan hath desired, &c.}* As if he said, I have indeed told you of a kingdom, which I intend you: but be yet not secure, who have a fierce and subtle adversary to encounter with: you are the good wheat; for many trials must you look, to cleanse you that you may not be cast away with the unhappy chaff to unquenchable fire, and Matt. 3. *and thou Simon Peter*, Satan has desired to overthrow you, but he shall only cleanse and refine you, by humbling you in the knowledge of your infirmity.

*{sift you as wheat}* This *σινιάσαι* *Suidas* interprets by *σῆσαι*, *κοσκινίσαι*, *θορυβῆσαι* *ταράξαι*, and *πειράσαι*: that is to shake to and again, as in a sieve to trouble, terrify and discompose as the tumultuous waters in a storm; here is the malice of Satan principally aiming at the ruin of the best, he passes by wicked men, as his own slaves and vassals of whom he is already sure: *eos quærit dejicere quos videt stare. Cyprian, lib. 3. ep. 1.* He is ambitious to cast them down whom he sees standing. See Isaiah 30. 28. Amos 9. 9.

**V. 32. ***{I have prayed for thee}* Not for Peter only does Christ mediate, but for all the faithful, John 17. 20. and therefore he will not suffer us to be tempted above that we are able: whoever therefore fails not in tribulation, *ab illo se non dubitet adjuvari. Prosper. con. Collat.* let him not doubt but Christ solicits for him and helps him; so admirable is God's patience *qua contemptus parcit*—he spares even being contemned, never can he want mercy, *quando homini & indulgentia consulit, & flagello.* Seeing he provides for man, both in indulgence, and the rod. *ib. Prosp. sent.*

**V. 33. ***{I am ready to goe with thee, &c.}* Peter ignorant of his own infirmity, is thus confident; but having learned by experience that the best meaning man easily falls into the most grievous sin, if God take away his sustaining hand, he afterward answered more modestly, *Lord, thou knowest all things, thou knowest that I love thee*, John 21. it is a very hard matter to excel in any gifts and not to be too confident thereof: which is the cause why God usually humbles those by some great trials whom he furnishes with gifts extraordinary, as Paul also acknowledges, 2 Cor. 12. 7.

**V. 34. ***{And he said}* Matthew 26. 34. Mark 14. 30. John 13. 38.

**V. 35. ***{He said unto them}* Matth. 10. 9. Luke 10. 4.

*{sent you without purse}* He compares his absence from the disciples before his passion, with that which followed after his passion; noting the variation of the times; as if he had said, the instruction which I gave you for the execution of your first commission (Matt. 10. 9, 10.) was but temporal, and for that time only observable: at that time though you take no viands with you, you wanted nothing, for I did extraordinarily provide for you: by inclining their hearts to whom I sent you, to supply you with all necessaries: I then complied with the weakness of children, instancing my miraculous providence: now the time requires that you be armed to encounter many difficulties, like men, to give example to succeeding ministers, who are not always to expect or depend on miracles for their supplies in things temporal: and this which he says concerning a purse or sword verse 36. is to intimate their necessities and dangers imminent, which he speaking of, allays their unseasonable ambition: as if he said, you contend about superiority; but you must all undergo many necessities and dangers: thus he warns and arms them against imminent afflictions; especially, with spiritual arms, wherewith external means are not to be neglected.

**V. 37. ***{Must yet be accomplished in me}* That is, I who am your Lord, am now to suffer for the redemption of man, according as it was foretold by the prophets.

*{he was reckoned among the transgressors}* Isaiah 53. 12. Mark 15. 28. So they apprehended him as a malefactor with swords and staves, and they crucified him among thieves.

*{the things concerning me}* That is, which the prophets have written, or the law prefigured concerning me.

*{have an end}* All is, and shortly shall be fulfilled, for the accomplishment of your redemption, so that it should be an argument of your confidence, not of fear and dejection. (See Matt. 27. 38. Mark 15. 28.) seeing that Christ does indeed fulfill all that was foretold of him: thus Christ confirms the authority of Scriptures, the scope whereof is to bring us to Christ, eternal life and true happiness, John 17. 3.

**V. 38. ***{Here are two swords}* This said they, not understanding of what armor he spoke.

*{it is enough}* Thereby teaching them that he spoke not of a bodily armor, for which, against a world of enemies, many thousand swords had been too few.

**V. 39. ***{And he came out}* Matth. 26. 36. Mark 14. 32. John 18. 1. He went away out of the house, in which he had eaten the Passover, toward the garden where he was to begin his passion.

*{as he was wont}* Unto *Gethsemane*, Matthew 26. 36. Mark 14. 32. which is a village at the foot of the mount of *Olives* (*Jerom. de loc. Hebraic.*) whereto he was wont to resort with his disciples, John 18. 2.

**V. 40. ***{And when he was}* Matt. 26. 41. Mark 14. 38.

*{pray that ye enter not into temptation}* As if he said, if you are not solicitous for me now entering into my sad passion: yet be careful for yourselves: you also are in present danger of being surprised with a fearful temptation, (of which, verse 31. See 1 Peter 5. 8.) to enter into temptation, here imports to be overcome thereof: we being not able of ourselves to resist, are to beg assistance and deliverance at God's hands in vigilant and intentive prayer.

**V. 41. ***{He was withdrawn from them}* The word *ἀπεσπάσθη* here used, signifies he was pulled or drawn away: it is of *σπάω* *traho*, or *evello*: the Syriac interprets it by *פרק* *perach*, which among other significations, is *seperare, segregare, se secedere*, and thence *פריק* *parich. remotus, absens, distans*. He withdrew himself a small distance from his disciples, that he might pray alone: possibly as men in bitterness of spirit, and anguish of mind, use to turn away, or withdraw from company. (See Gen. 43. 30. Gen. 42. 24.) to give their passion freer vent: so Christ having taken on him all our infirmities without sin, being now to wrestle with his heavy agony and deep sense of his Father's anger against sinners, in whose place he now stood, withdrew a little from his disciples, lest he should have hazarded their yet weak faith, by their too near sense and apprehension of his bitter sorrows.

**V. 42. ***{Remove this cup from me}* This is a Hebraism *כוס* *cos*, by a metaphor signified that condition which was prepared, and imminent, whether good or evil. See Psalm 11. 6. Isai. 51. 17. Lam. 4. 41. Matt. 26. 22. Psal. 64. 8. Psalm 16. 5. and 73. 10. Ezekiel 23. 33. Zach. 12. 2.

*{not my will, but thine be done}* As God, he was of the same will with the Father and Holy Spirit: but as he was man subject to all our infirmities (sin only excepted) so was he of another, yet not contrary will, because by the unmeasured grace which he had received in his divine unction, John 3. 34.) he subjected that human will (naturally desiring to preserve the humanity) unto the will of the Father, even unto death; teaching us this precept, chap. 11. 2. by his own present example, and as *Bernard*, says, *de quat. mod. orandi: Deo magis committere, quam temere, aliquid apud nos definire*, to commit our desires and necessities to God, rather than rashly determine anything of ourselves. We may and often err in petitioning, God cannot err in giving: we know not what to pray as we ought, Rom. 8. but God knows what is best to bestow on us: what is good for the sick, the physician better knows than the patient himself.

**V. 43. ***{Strengthening him}* Not by communication of any virtue; but by presenting his humanity the certain assurance of his speedy victory and glory, while the Deity, as it were hid his light and comfortable presence.

**V. 44. ***{In an agony}* That is, a vehement conflict in his soul, through the deepest sense of his Father's wrath against sinners, for whom he now stood as a surety and Redeemer, 2 Corinthians 5. 21.

*{prayed more earnestly}* More fervently, for he wrestled not only with the terrors of death as other mere men are wont to do (for then many martyrs might have seemed more constant and resolute than he) but with the terrible justice of God pouring out his anger on sinners, than which nothing more dreadful can be thought of; for he bore the burden of all our sins, Isai. 53. 4, 5, 6. and whatever punishments were thereto due; this conflict therefore, and passion of Christ is the sum of our consolation: he having therein fully overcome death and hell for us, and the fear and horror thereof: so that all this lessened not his majesty, but increased our assurance of reconciliation with God and salvation with Christ risen from the dead, God's justice, which we have provoked, being fully satisfied by the inestimable merits of Christ's passion.

*{great drops of blood}* *θρόμβοι* *αἵματος*, clotted, or congealed blood: *Dioscor. l. 3. c. 44.* *θρόμβους* *αἵματος* *διαλύει*, it dissolves congealed blood. So *Suidas* *θρόμβοι* *ἁλῶν*—*παχὺς* *ἅλς*, congealed salt, the Syr. gives it, *droppings down of blood*, that is in greater and grosser drops distilling.

**V. 45. ***{Sleeping for sorrow}* It seems they were stupefied with a heavy grief, for that which he had told them: otherwise, sorrow and care are usually sleepless: mark how little truth there is in frail flesh and blood: they who had boldly promised, (and doubtless meant no less) to go to prison and death for Christ, could not so much as watch with him one hour in so grievous an affliction.

**V. 46. ***{Rise and pray}* Matt. 26. 41. See verse 40. As if he said, it is now no season to sleep in the midst of these imminent and now instant dangers, arise and watch in earnest prayer, that you fall not into temptation, but that God may be pleased to hear and deliver you.

**V. 47. ***{Behold a multitude}* Matt. 26. 47. Mark 14. 43. John 18. 13. Matt. 26. 47. a great multitude, armed with swords and staves, John 18. 3. says, *a band of men and officers, &c.*

*{to kiss him}* Which was their manner of friendly salutation. Luke 7. 45. Judas had given them that sign by which they should apprehend Jesus, Matt. 26. 48.

**V. 48. ***{Betrayest thou the Son of man with a kiss}* Do you clothe your treason with shows of love? Judas would after such evident detection, yet veil his treason; therefore he would not act it openly: possibly he might fear, lest otherwise he might escape their hands, as he had sometimes done: therefore he makes semblance of love and friendship: but Christ discovers his vain hypocrisy, under which they could not have taken him, had he not offered himself to that passion whereby he was to redeem man.

**V. 49. ***{shall we smite with the sword}* It seems they were confident of extraordinary assistance, who dared draw two swords against a multitude; but understood not that Christ's kingdom was spiritual, not secular; and so not to be defended with arms and violence, but with spiritual armor, 2 Cor. 10. 4. Eph. 6. 13, 14, etc.

**V. 50. ***{One of them smote}* Matt. 26. 51. Mark 14. 47. This was Peter, John 18. 10. who out of zeal to defend his Master, stayed not to expect Christ's answer, but presently struck. See Matthew 26. 51.

*{the servant of the high Priest}* This was Malcus, John 18. 10.

**V. 51. ***{Suffer ye thus far}* Meaning that they might take him, and do what God had appointed. Mat. 26. 52. Christ bids him put up his sword, intimating that neither *Peter*, nor any other private person or persons might take up the sword, to defend the cause of Christ. 1. Because the *jus gladii* belongs not to any private person, but to public authority, Rom. 13. 4. much less to the minister. Secondly, because they who smite with the sword, shall perish with the sword. Mat. 26. 52. Thirdly, because God's will must be done in our sufferings, John 18. 11. Lastly, God can, and ever will as he knows best, defend his own cause: he has legions of angels at command. Mat. 26. 53.

**V. 52. ***{captains of the Temple}* See verse 4. they that commanded the guard of the temple. See on John 18. 12. Acts 4. 1.

**V. 53. ***{But this is your hour}* The time wherein God has permitted you to rage against me; and wherein the Devil the prince of darkness, endeavors by you to destroy me, who am the light of the world.

**V. 54. ***{Then took they him}* Matthew 26. 57. Mark 14. 66. John 18. 12. Then when he who offered himself a sacrifice for our redemption permitted them so to do: so blind were they that they were nothing moved by the sense of that power of Jesus, which in their approach cast them to the ground, John 18. 6. nor his present healing Malcus' ear, to consider either his mercy to save, or divine power to destroy, and, if he pleased, to frustrate all their power and endeavors against him.

**V. 55. ***{When they had kindled a fire}* Mat. 26. 69. Mark 14. 66. John 18. 25. In the midst of the hall, that they might sit or stand about it, and warm themselves, who were to attend their masters coming out.

**V. 56. ***{A certain maid}* Mat. 26. 69. Mark 14. 66, 67. John 18. 17. See verse 60.

**V. 57. ***{Woman I know him not}* So dangerous a temptation is carnal fear: it was an undoubted effect of love that *Peter* followed Christ into that danger, as also of a violent perturbation of mind by fear, that he denied him: they that truly love God (by the grace of God) are yet of themselves infirm; because, yet, but partly flesh. Here were three immediate causes of Peter's failing. 1. Too much confidence in himself. 2. The company of Christ's enemies; with whom it is very difficult, not to fall: because he seems to correct their error, who dissents from their practice. 3. The wisdom of flesh, here suggesting to himself, what he once spoke to Christ speaking of this his passion. Mat. 16. 22. *ἵλεώς* *σοι*, *regard*, or *favor yourself*.

**V. 58. ***{Man, I am not}* Once fallen, he could not presently recover: the falls of a sinner take away the present sense of sin, and affect the mind with such a stupor, that therein it cannot understand the evil done: so easy is it for the best to fall, so hard to rise again.

**V. 59. ***{About the space of an hour after, another}* Satan (who never wants variety of employers) doubles his charges, and violently follows his mischievous advantages upon those whom he casts down: it should enforce the Apostle's admonition. 1 Cor. 10. 12. *Wherefore let him that thinketh he standeth, take heed lest he fall.*

**V. 60. ***{Peter said, man, I know not what thou sayest}* Here seems much difficulty (which has wrested some learned pens into very dangerous inconveniences) in the reconciliation of the seeming differences between the Evangelists, relating the same story of Christ's convention before the high priest, and Peter's denial, but with some different circumstances: as for example.

1. The three first Evangelists relate all as acted before Caiphas , to whom Christ was led. Mat. 26. 57.
2. That Peter followed him hither . Mat. 26. 58. and warmed himself there, Mark 14. 54. Luke 22. 55.
3. That they struck Christ in Caiphas' presence. Mark 14. 65. Mat. 26. 64. and here verse 63.
4. That two maids accused Peter, Mat. 26. 69, 71. Mark 14. 66, 69. but here verse 58. another man said: which was the second charge.
5. That John 18. 24. saying, Annas had sent him bound to Caiphas ; yet he says verse 25. Simon Peter stood and warmed himself : and then upon a second charge denied Christ, and upon a third verse 26, 27. that he denied a third time.

For the reconciliation of these seeming difficulties, we must know. 1. That all the word of God is infallibly true, and such as there cannot be any error or falsehood in it. 2. That one of the Evangelists recites some things, which another, or all the rest omitted. So that, all that which they or any of them wrote, is certainly true, though neither any one, nor all of them wrote all that which was true. See John 20. 3. and 21. 25. for instance: John wrote concerning Christ's being carried to *Annas first*, (John 18. 13.) who was father-in-law to *Caiphas* (which *Caiphas* was high priest, that is suffragan to *Annas* the high priest. (See on John 18. 13. the same year.) this, the other three Evangelists omit: but relate the main actions, without this circumstance. 3.
