They also relate Peter's following of Christ into the high priest's hall: Mark and this Evangelist, speak of warming himself; but omit the place, *Annas' hall*, which John mentions: also John speaks of his standing and warming himself, which circumstance appertains to that part of the story which was acted in *Annas' hall*; except we shall say that Malcus' charge was against him standing by a second mentioned fire in *Caiphas' hall* also: there being the same reason for a fire in that place as well as the other. 4. That John says Christ was struck in *Annas'* house, John 18. 22. but the rest relate it as done in *Caiphas' hall*: either these Evangelists observe not the circumstances and order of the story (omitting, that he was first carried to *Annas*, which John relates) contented to historify the things themselves: or else, which is possible, they did twice strike Jesus: so John shows the occasion was, their ill taking the manner of his answering the high priest *Annas*, John 18. 22. but the other Evangelists relate to the matter of his answer (Mat. 26. 64, etc. Mark 14. 62, etc.) as the end of their smiting him, and the occasion subordinate thereto: that is, their blindfolding him, and bidding him prophesy who smote him, because the people esteemed him a prophet. 5. That Matthew and Mark say *two maids* accused Peter; the first, and second time, but Luke 22. 58. it is said, that first a maid, and then a man charged him: both are true; and John 18. 25. it is clearly reconciled, where mentioning the second charge he says, *εἶπον*, *they said*; that is the maid, and a man at least, it may be with some others also; so Matthew and Mark, mention the woman in the second charge: and this Evangelist mentions the man: but John signifies both, if not divers others. 6. That after John said *Annas sent him bound to Caiphas*) he records the second denial, it makes not that this second denial was at *Caiphas'* (the surrogates) house: for who knows not the many *anticipations* in sacred histories; wherein that is not first set down, which was first acted; as so, that is not always to be understood, to be first in time, which is first set down in the series of a story: understand therefore the two first denials to have been at *Annas'* house, and the last at *Caiphas'* house, whence he went out and wept bitterly; for between the two first, and the last denial, verse 59. it evidently appears, that there was about an hour's space: and this last denial was occasioned by *Malcus* (whose ear *Peter* had cut off) his charge; which the other Evangelists omit, as also their carrying him to *Annas*, and his sending him bound to *Caiphas*; which John records, John 18. 13.

*{the Cock crew}* Peter could not take warning by the word and premonition of Christ himself: but hearing the cock crow, he came to himself and remembered: that stayed him not from sin; this occasioned his repentance: in the first, Christ left him to himself, the sense and sad experience of his own disability to resist a temptation: in the second, he minded him of the signal whereof he foretold him. The most powerful means cannot awake the sinner, until God's remembering Spirit make them effectual; the least shall, where he so does.

**V. 61. ***{Then the Lord turned back and looked on Peter}* He looked back as *Theophylact* says, *ἀγαθῷ* *βλέμματι* with a benign aspect: then Peter remembered; forgetting Christ's words he fell; but recalling them to mind, he repented.

*{before the cock crow}* Mat. 26. 34. John 13. 38.

**V. 62. ***{went out and wept &c.}* This was the effect of his sight, detestation of his sin, and grief that he had so offended a gracious God: we read that he wept, we read not that he satisfied: God accepts the sole merit of Christ for a full satisfaction for our sins, but yet he justly requires our repentance, that his mercy may have place: repentance is the key to the kingdom of grace. See Mat. 3. 2. Mat. 4. 17. Such is God's mercy and patience (as *Prosper* forecited says) *quia parcit contemptus, parcit etiam negatus*; wherein being contemned, yea being denied, he spares, and forgives.

**V. 63. ***{mocked him}* Mat. 26. 67. Mark 14. 65. John 18. 22. *παίζειν*, whereof the word here used is, properly signifies, to jest childishly; or foolishly; or that which the Hebrews express by *קלס* *kalas* which in its first signification importing, *to praise*, by an *antiphrasin* signifies, in *Hithpael, derisit, subsannavit*, he jeered, which is a scoffing by seeming to admire, praise or approve him whom he derides: or by some disgraceful term, as 2 Kings 2. 23. so *עלל* *halal*, among other significations, imports a doing or saying something in scorn and contempt: so says *Theophylact*, *ὁ* *τῶν* *προφητῶν* *δεσπότης* *ὡς* *ψευδοπροφήτης* *χλευάζεται*, the Lord of the prophets, is mocked as a false prophet; or an impostor.

**V. 64. ***{prophesy, who is it that smote thee}* With these scoffing blasphemies they insult; because the people honored him as a prophet.

**V. 65. ***{blasphemously spake they, &c.}* See on verse 70. Possibly some saying that he was a glutton, wine-drinker, and friend of publicans and sinners, as chap. 7. 35. Others that he had formerly cast out devils by *Beelzebub*, (chap. 11. 15.) who now failed him, so that he could non deliver himself from punishment, now they had apprehended him: another, that he was no prophet who would not foresee his own dangers, and deliver himself from them; that he had but deceived foolish people, etc. Satan was witty to invent blasphemous suggestions, and they malicious to apprehend and utter them; all which Jesus heard, and patiently endured in his sufferings for our sins.

**V. 66. ***{As soon as it was day}* Mat. 27. 1. Mark 16. 1. John 18. 28. So industrious is malice, Satan will not let the wicked, his servants, sleep when any mischief is to be done, but sits heavy on their eyes at church, or when they should rise to go to it, for fear they might be moved to faith and repentance, and so he should lose them, and their service.

*{into their council}* Their consistory, or *Sanhedrim*; which sat about matters of religion. See Mat. 5. 22. Mat. 26. 3. Thus would they color their malice with shows of justice and legal proceeding.

**V. 67. ***{art thou the Christ?}* The expected Messias, promised by the prophets; the King of Israel; as your disciples believe? thus they hypocritically and captiously ask, not with any intent to believe the truth; but to find something whereof to accuse, and condemn him as an enemy of *Cæsar*, or blasphemer of God.

*{If I tell you, you will not believe}* You, who would not believe for so many miracles as you have seen me do, will not believe my words; unbelievers are sometimes importunate to hear the word, which shall serve only to condemn their disobedience and unbelief. See Jer. 42. 2, 3. Jer. 43. 2. and 44. 16.

**V. 68. ***{you will not answer me}* See chap. 20. 3, 4. 7. 17. 44. you will not be instructed, being resolved, not to put my cause on truth, and evincing reason, but violence.

*{nor let me go}* You are already resolved to destroy me.

**V. 69. ***{Hereafter shall the Son of man}* Mat. 26. 64. Mark 14. 62. At his second coming, he whom you now judge and condemn as a malefactor, shall judge you; though you destroy this life, he shall live again in heavenly majesty and glory.

*{at the right hand}* In the power and glory of his heavenly kingdom. Heb. 1. 3. and 8. 1.

**V. 70. ***{Art thou then the Son of God}* As if in blasphemous scorn they said; *thou*, carpenter's son; *thou* friend of publicans and sinners; *thou* profaner of Sabbaths: *thou* the Son of God? See verse 65.

*{ye say that I am}* That is, I am. Mark 14. 62. or, you say the truth, for I am so.

**V. 71. ***{What need we any further, &c.}* The Syriac reads, *what need we* (*תוב*) *again*; as the Hebr. *עוד* *hod* imports, any addition, or a second time; for they had in the night sought for witness, against him, but found none: for *many bare false witnesse against him but their witnesse agreed not together*. Mark 14. 55, 56. As if they said, we had before, and have enough against him now; for he professes himself the Son of God: the wicked think the very profession of the truth of Christ evidence enough to condemn the professors thereof.

## CHAPTER 23

**Verse 1. ***{And the whole multitude}* Mat. 27. 2. Mark 15. 1. John 18. 28. When they supposed they had enough now to persuade *Pilate* to condemn him to death, which themselves, had great will, but no power to do, in prosecution of their malicious desire to destroy him; the multitude of those that were in the council, and the attendants thereon (the priests, elders, scribes, etc.) none would be absent.

*{unto Pilate}* The Roman deputy, and chief governor of that province; who had power of judicature in matters capital, which the Romans permitted not the Jews.

**V. 2. ***{perverting the nation}* See Acts 17. 7. By new pernicious doctrines seducing the people, persuading them against the divine law, and institutions of God by Moses.

*{forbidding to give tribute to Caesar}* This was a false calumny, as appears, Mat. 17. 27. & 22. 21. (as was the former) and here chap. 20. 25. but this they said, that they might engage *Pilate* the Roman deputy, in their impious quarrel. See Isaiah 53. 2. John 6. 15.

*{Christ a King}* Gr. *an anointed King*; Syriac that he was the King Messias; they took that for granted (which was most untrue) that the Messias should have an external and secular kingdom, glorious for wealth, armies and victories: and that under him they should be delivered from all other powers, or jurisdictions; by this complaint to Pilate, they labored to intimate, that the interest of Cæsar was highly concerned, by Jesus professing himself to be a king, and the Messias, or by any of the Jews believing the same.

**V. 3. ***{Pilate asked him}* Mat. 27. 11. Mark 15. 2. John 18. 33.

*{art thou the King of the Jews?}* He asked him not whether he perverted the nation, or forbade men to pay tribute to *Cæsar*: it is likely that he being a vigilant deputy, had his intelligence and information concerning Christ: the rather for that the Herodians went with the Pharisees to catch him in this point. See Mat. 22. 16, etc. who, if he had denied the right of *Cæsar*, would quickly have informed against him: nor does he mention the third cavil, as suspecting him of competition for his deputyship; because he knew him to be poor, unarmed, and a contemner of secular honor; but to satisfy the impious Jews, lest by any malicious complaint to *Cæsar*, they should have begotten him trouble.

*{thou sayest it}* See on John 18. 37. I have no need to affirm it, seeing you say it; you answer yourself for me.

**V. 4. ***{Then said Pilate}* After he had examined him in the Pretory he gives the Jews there attending, this answer at the door; I find no fault in him worthy of death, as you suggest.

**V. 5. ***{they were more fierce}* They grew more violent: as the Syriac has it, *they cryed out upon him*: thinking by violence to prevail, seeing by right and equity they could not.

*{beginning from Galilee}* They mention Galilee, partly to mind him of the sedition there formerly bred: and partly to intimate that *Herod* might find some dangerous advantage against him, in case he should not hearken to their informations concerning the maintenance of *Cæsar's* right.

**V. 6. ***{a Galilean}* That is, a native, or an inhabitant of Galilee, and so of *Herod's* jurisdiction.

**V. 7. ***{he sent him to Herod}* As to acquit himself of him, so to ingratiate with *Herod*: this was *Herod Antipas*; the murderer of *John Baptist*. Chap. 3. 19, 20. *Herod* the great his son. Mat. 2.

*{was at Jerusalem at that time}* In those days of the Passover, *Herod* was at Jerusalem, at the feast. See Exod. 23. 17.

**V. 8. ***{he had heard many things of him}* See on Mat. 14. 1.

*{hoped to have seen some miracle}* He rejoiced that *Pilate* so much honored him, as to send him one that had done so many admirable works: as also in hope to satisfy his own curiosity, in seeing some miracle done: so do impious men abuse the holy power of God, as if it were some scenical spectacle, ordained for admiration, and pastime.

**V. 9. ***{he answered him nothing}* He knew *Herod* asked not with any intent to be converted; but for his curiosity, which Christ would not satisfy.

**V. 10. ***{accused him}* Wickedness is ever jealous: they fearing lest Christ might satisfy, and ingratiate with *Herod*, and so escape their fury who had resolved to destroy him: they repeat to *Herod* those things whereof they had accused him before *Pilate*: the more admirable was Jesus' present silence, verse 9. who neither by the presence of so great a prince, nor the importunate malice of his enemies, was moved to answer anything.

**V. 11. ***{a gorgeous robe}* Rich and honorable clothes, which they put on him in scorn. Gr. *λαμπρὰν* glittering, shining, or costly, the Syriac gives it *זחוריתא* *zechuritho*, scarlet, *χλαμύδα* *κοκκίνην*, Mat. 27. 28. *a skarlet robe*. So Hebr. 9. 19. *skarlet wool*; all this in derision, because Jesus would not satisfy *Herod's* vain desires, in showing some sign; had he found him guilty, he would otherwise have proceeded against him; this injury they did him, because the Jews accused him of affecting a kingdom.

**V. 12. ***{Pilate and Herod were made friends}* It seems their former contest was about jurisdiction. See chap. 13. 1. which *Pilate* in this instance salving, ambitious *Herod* was satisfied: cursed friendship and unhappy peace is that, which is made to the injury of Christ: nor was this blasphemous scoffing of *Herod* long unrevenged; as *Josephus* writes *Antiq. Jud. l. 18. c. 9.* being accused to *Caius Cæsar*, he was by him put out of his kingdom, or *Tetrarchie*, and his goods given to *Agrippa*; himself condemned to perpetual banishment into *Lions in France* (some say into *Spain*) he died with grief.

**V. 13. ***{called together the chief Priests}* Their *Sanhedrin* or great council.

**V. 14. ***{no fault in this man}* This heathen's justifying Christ, condemned the Jews' malice against him.

**V. 16. ***{I will therefore chastise him, &c.}* Mat. 27. 26. Mark 15. 15. John 19. 1. Offenses not capital, the Roman presidents used so to punish with stripes, or scourging: this *Pilate* would have done to appease the Jews' rage: he had not learned, that we may not do evil, that good may come thereof. Rom. 3. 8. much less may we do evil, to avoid evil: he ought to have defended the innocent, and to have delivered him: but once declining from justice, he fell to that impious compliance with the Jews' malice, which was against justice, and the testimony of his own conscience.

**V. 17. ***{for of necessity}* Of custom granted them by the Romans. See Mat. 27. 15. Mark 15. 6. John 18. 39.

**V. 18. ***{they cried out all}* That is, the generality: Israel the now peculiar people of God, rages and thirsts for the holy blood of Christ; *Pilate* the heathen abhors such cruel injustice. See 1. How easily a holy people may be misled, or degenerate. 2. What trust there is in fickle multitudes, who so quickly can change their *Hosanna's* to *crucifige's*. 3. Whether enraged malice will carry men, making professors of truth do that which sober heathens loathe and are ashamed of?

**V. 19. ***{for a certain sedition, &c.}* See on Mat. 27. 16.

**V. 20. ***{willing to release Jesus}* See on Mat. 27. 17.

**V. 21. ***{but they cried saying crucify him}* The vehemency of their impious desires, causes them to double their cries, to make the voice seem the stronger: thus wicked men use to second their sins with greater and greater wickedness.

**V. 22. ***{the third time}* *Peter*, for fear denied Christ three times, and yet repented; *Pilate* three times justified Christ, and yet for popular favor condemned him: it may teach us, neither to despair if we repent, nor to presume though we have begun well.

**V. 23. ***{chief Priests prevailed}* These voices overswayed *Pilate's* conscience. Some Greek copies have not *καὶ* *τῶν* *ἀρχιερέων*—but the best generally have, the Syr. has it, *& ingens erat vox eorum, & summorum sacerdotum*, noting a danger of, and propensity to some uproar, if they were not satisfied with the blood of Jesus.

**V. 24. ***{Pilate gave sentence}* Or *consented*: neither according to law, conscience, nor custom, but to please the people: possibly he thought to avoid an insurrection hereby, but this injustice followed him: for about seven years after, being banished to *Lyons*, and reduced to miserable straits, he slew himself.

**V. 25. ***{Jesus to their will}* That is, to crucify and do what they would with him. See Mat. 27. 26.

**V. 26. ***{as they led him away}* Mat. 27. 32. Mark 15. 21. Out of the city to *Calvary*. See Hebr. 13. 12.

*{they laid hold upon Simon a Cyrenian}* That is, the soldiers took him to carry the cross of Christ; there are many *Simons* mentioned in the Gospel; *Simon Peter*. Mat. 10. 2. *Simon the Canaanite*. Mat. 13. 4. *Simon the leper*. Mat. 26. 6. *Simon* father of *Judas Iscariot*. John 13. 2. *Simon the tanner*. Acts 9. 43. *Simon Zelotes* Acts 1. 13. *Simon Magus*. Acts 8. 9. *Simon Niger*. Acts 13. 1. *Simon* of *Cyrene*; there were two cities of this name; one in Libya. Acts 2. 10. the other in Assyria, called *קיר* *Kir*. 2 Kings 16. 9. Amos 9. 7. that is, as *Hierom* there interprets, *Cyrene*. See *Plin. l. 5. c. 5. Strabo. l. 17. Ammian. Marcellin. l. 22. hom. de Pastor. ascript. Augustino.* Here *Simon* the Cyrenian; that is, *ἀπὸ* *Κυρήνης* *Suidas*. *Simon* a man of Cyrene, father of *Alexander* and *Rufus*. Mark 15. 21. Christ first bore his cross, but as they went between the *Pretorie* and *Calvary*, they met with this *Simon* coming out of the country. Mark 15. 21. and they compelled him to bear the cross of Jesus. *Hierom* on Mat. 27. 32. Christ being wearied with watching, stripes, and so long attendance on the varieties of judges, was not able any further to bear such a weight, as being man subject to all infirmities, without sin, to suffer for man's redemption; therefore the next seeming-strong man they meet, they command him to carry it for him: so he first set up the trophy of his cross, and then delivered it to the martyrs to erect it, said *Ambros. in Luc. l. 10.* this came to the lot of *Simon* father of *Alexander* and *Rufus* (which were either Christ's disciples, or men well known of them; for of such he seems to speak.) Such was the unbridled liberty of the Roman soldiers, under whose command the Jews then were; which their intestine seditions brought upon them by the quarrels of ambitious *Aristobulus* and *Hyrcanus*, calling in foreign forces to aid them: so that they who would, according to God's ordinance maintain the sweet bond of mutual peace, nor obey their superiors, now suffered the grievous insolencies of strangers, ruling over them, in an arbitrary way: the like dissension in the republics of Greece and Rome, have found the like event: we have smarted for the same fault, God grant we do not again.

**V. 27. ***{and of women which bewailed him}* A great multitude followed him: some to insult, some of those of whom the Pharisees spoke, John 7. 45. among them many women, some who believed in him: and because they are usually more compassionate, them Christ admonishes to weep for themselves, for the imminent destruction which would be most grievous to them.

**V. 28. ***{daughters of Jerusalem}* Women of Jerusalem; it is a Hebraism. Frequent in Scripture. See 2 Kings 19. 21. Isa. 37. 22. Lam. 2. 13, 15. Mich. 4. 8, etc.

*{weep not for me; but weep for yourselves}* He relates in this prohibition, as to the supreme cause of his present passion (God's counsel and his compliance thereto) so also to the end thereof, God's glory; his exaltation to God's right hand, and man's salvation hereby accomplished: in his admonition he points at the sad calamity in their siege by the sword, pestilence, and famine, so grievous, that mothers should eat their own dear children.

**V. 29. ***{The days are coming}* In the destruction of Jerusalem by the Roman. You now insulting over my afflictions, think yourselves safe and well, because your governors have their will to destroy me, but alas a terrible revenge follows, and is now at hand.

*{Blessed are the barren}* He further describes the bitter sorrow of that tender sex in public calamities, more afflicted with their helpless children's sufferings, than their own.

**V. 30. ***{then they shall begin, &c.}* See Isaiah 2. 19. Hos. 10. 8. Rev. 6. 16. that is, then they shall too late say; in their fear and inevitable calamity.

*{fall on us}* He expresses the passion of men in extreme agonies, wishing rather to die speedily, than to live in the lingering misery of famine: or desiring rather any ways to hide themselves than to be exposed to public ruin.

**V. 31. ***{For if they do these things, &c.}* He compares himself to a green tree, less combustible and apt to take fire? and the Jews to a dry tree: as if he said; if so severe be the fire of God's judgment, and the rage of men permitted to execute the same on me, who have no sin, and matter of punishment in myself, but only as I stand in the place of sinners: what shall become of those vessels of wrath, (Rom. 9. 22.) the Jews who have in them such abundant matter of condemnation? in this the more, because they now crucify their Savior and Lord of life. See Isaiah 5. 24. and 33. 11. & 47. 14. Obad. 1. 18. Mal. 4. 1. the sum of this proverbial manner of speaking is, if judgment be so heavy on me, in whom they can find no fault, what shall they suffer who are the fuel of God's wrath? 1 Pet. 4. 17.

**V. 32. ***{and there were also}* Mat. 27. 38. Isaiah 53. 12.

*{two other malefactors}* Two other men which were malefactors; so that not only on the cross, which *Mark* notes, chap. 15. 28. but also in his leading out, was fulfilled that which Jesus foretold, chap. 22. 37. out of Isaiah 53. 12. and he was reckoned among the transgressors.

**V. 33. ***{is called Calvary}* Or *the place of a skull*, Mat. 27. 33. Mark 15. 22. John 19. 17. See note there, and *Drus. ad voc. N. T.*

*{there they crucified him}* It was then in use the most ignominious death. See Hebr. 2.

**V. 34. ***{Father forgive them}* Acts 7. 60. Acts 3. 17. Being now in such intense torments, so pursued in death, with the implacable malice of his undeserved enemies, before he provided for his weeping mother (John 19. 26, 27.) he prays for his enemies: there is no temporal thing of so great importance as saving of souls: Christ taught us to love our enemies, not only by precept, Mat. 5. 44. but here, by his most admirable example thus began he on the cross, to show himself not only a Redeemer by his bloodshed, but also a Mediator by his intercession; the effect of this prayer appeared, Acts 2. 36. in Peter's sermon (addressed to them who had crucified the Lord of life) and the conversion of his auditors added to the church that day. See also Acts 4. 4. Acts 6. 7. So that by the mercy of God, they who in their ignorance impiously shed the blood of Jesus, in the mystery, through faith, drank the same to their salvation.

**V. 35. ***{derided him}* See on ch. 16. 14. This was an addition of sorrow to his passion, that all sorts of Jews despised him, insulting over him: all this was that our sin (who fell by *Adam's* ambition) might thus be expiated, by the bitter ignominy of Christ's passion.

*{the chosen of God}* This is an epithet and title of the *Messias*. See Isaiah 42. 2.

**V. 36. ***{offering him vinegar}* Mixed with myrrh and gall. See on John 19. 29. herein was fulfilled that which was spoken. Psal. 69. 21.

**V. 37. ***{if thou be the king of the Jews}* That is, the *Messias* whom the Jews expected to deliver them: this also they insultingly speak, impliedly charging him with vain ambition and usurpation of regality, and name of a king; being (as they thought) not able to deliver himself from the power of the Romans.

**V. 38. ***{a superscription}* See on Mat. 27. 37. A title, or cause of his death, *Augustine epist. 8.* So Mark 15. 26. and Mat. 27. 37. to make the spectators believe that he was therefore condemned and crucified, because he affected the kingdom of *Israel*. *Pilate* finding no fault in him, set down this in place of an accusation, which yet the Jews (suspecting it, might reflect on their reputation) quarreled at, John 19. 21.

*{Greek, and Latin, and Hebrew}* That the thing which concerned all nations, might thereby be divulged to all. To the East by the *Hebrew*, to the South by the *Greek*, to the West by the *Latine*: this was necessary in regard of the present confluence of people to the feast at *Jerusalem*, from all nations, John 12. 20. There were proselytes of all nations, and Jews of the dispersion among the Gentiles, who (though born of Jewish parents) yet spoke the tongue of that people among whom they had been born and bred. See Acts 2. 8.

**V. 39. ***{were hanged}* Being nailed to the cross, as the Roman manner of execution then was.

**V. 40. ***{doest not thou fear God}* Does not the punishment you suffer, cause you to fear God, whose hand has thus overtaken you? It is a desperate condition in the bitter effects of sin, not to be sensible of the cause thereof. However the prosperous securely and inconsiderately contemn the judgments of God, yet do not you now under the heavy burden thereof, fear an intolerable accession thereto, whom neither present torments, nor bitter death, under whose arrests you are, anything amends? It was a great addition to *Asa's* sin, that in his disease he sought not unto the Lord—2 Chron. 26. 12.

**V. 41. ***{but this man hath done nothing amiss}* *Greg. hom. 22. in Evang.* He suffered without sin, that which we suffer for, and with sin.

**V. 42. ***{Lord remember me}* When you come into your glory, receive me to mercy as one of yours. Remember not my sin (so Psal. 25. 7.) but accomplish your mercy which you have now begun to show me in my conversion. Remember me when you come into that state of blessedness which you have purchased, unto which you are going from the cross, that I may follow you into that blessedness: here was a rare and excellent faith apprehending the merit of Christ for his salvation: he believes him to be the Savior, whom men condemned as a malefactor, whom the tormenters crucified, the impious Jews derided, now dying, to man's sense, forsaken of God; in this doleful state and posture he believed him to be the holy Son of God, able to save in death; the disciples forsook him, *Peter* forswore all acquaintance with him: but this poor convert acknowledged him dying to be the Lord of life: true faith has piercing eyes, seeing the glory and kingdom of Christ through the dark ignominy of the cross.

**V. 43. ***{To day thou shalt be with me, &c.}* This day you shall be with me in heaven. See 2 Cor. 12. 2, 4. Rev. 2. 7. By the garden of Paradise where *Adam* was placed in his state of innocence; is signified here (as elsewhere) the state of eternal blessedness.

**V. 44. ***{about the sixth hour}* See on Mat. 27. 45. Mark 15. 33. About noon. His passion began about nine of the clock in the morning, which was their hour of prayer, and the ordinary hour of the morning sacrifice daily offered; and ended at the ninth hour, that is about three of the clock in the afternoon: at the time of the evening sacrifice, Mark 15. 25, 33. and this was the time for the Passover. By this time of the day God foreshowed the sufferings of Christ in time's evening (the last days. Hebr. 1. 2. 1 Pet. 1. 19, 20.) about the same time of the day when the Paschal Lamb usually died. See Exod. 12. 6. *בין* *הערבים* *between the two evenings*, that is as the 70 give it *πρὸς* *ἑσπέραν*, towards the evening, in the afternoon, before sunsetting. All the forenoon was counted morning, and all the afternoon evening: so their later evening began at sunsetting. Josh. 10. 26, 27.

*{over all the earth}* Or *land*.

**V. 45. ***{the vail of the temple was rent}* Syr. *the face of the port of the temple*. Gr. *καταπέτασμα* *τοῦ* *ναοῦ*, the *hanging*, which divided (as some think) the Holy of Holies, from the holy place: as others, with *Jerome*, the holy place into which the priests went to officiate, from the outward court: it is uncertain which was rent now. See *Is. Casaubon exerc. 16. N. 127. Joh. Drus. præt. l. 1. in Mat. 27. 51.* In this relation *Luke* prepossesses that part of the story, which should have fallen with Christ's giving up the ghost, Mat. 27. 50. omitting some things which the other Evangelists recite. See *Aug. de cons. Evang. l. 3. c. 19.*

**V. 46. ***{into thy hands I commend, &c.}* Psal. 31. 5. Now holy Father, that I have performed your will in all things for the redemption of your elect, it remains only that I commend my soul to your blessed keeping, and so die, the Hebrew word *אפקיד* in the Psalm signifies (as does also the Greek *παραθήσομαι*, by the 70. and so here used) so to commend as a thing safely deposited, 1 Pet. 4. 9. Luke 12. 48. 2 Tim. 2. 2. to teach us confidence in our deaths, when we commend our souls into the merciful hands of God, we but deposit them in the hand and custody of a faithful sequestration who will in the time appointed faithfully restore them again, and we shall rise to life eternal.

*{he gave up the ghost}* Mat. 7. 50. He died, it appears that this his bitter passion on the cross lasted full six hours. For Mark 15. 25. it is expressly said—it was the third hour, and they crucified him (the third according to the Jewish account; who began their day at sunrising (our six of the clock) so their third hour answered to our nine in the morning, the usual time of their morning sacrifice, at which hour Christ (thereby prefigured) began to be crucified, and Mark 15. 34, 37. It appears that about the ninth hour he died, that answered to our three of the clock in the afternoon when the evening sacrifice was killed: so that six hours (to wit from nine in the morning to three in the afternoon) he was on the cross in torment.

**V. 47. ***{when the centurion}* Mat. 27. 54. The Roman captain, who commanded the soldiers who crucified Christ.

*{he glorified God}* Convinced in conscience by that he saw, he confessed the truth. See Mat. 27. 54. See John 7. 17. confessing the truth in such case is to glorify God.

**V. 48. ***{smote their breasts}* Chap. 18. 13. As being touched at heart for that which they and the rest had done, in giving their voices to crucify Christ.

**V. 49. ***{all his acquaintance}* All who were present.

*{stood afar off}* For fear of the Jewish fury, they dared not appear near him: so was that fulfilled in him which was written, Psal. 88. 18.

**V. 50. ***{a man named Joseph}* Mat. 37. 57. Of *Arimathea*, to distinguish him from others named *Joseph*, Acts 1. 13. Mat. 1. 18, etc. Some think *Arimathea*, the same which is called *Ramah* in mount *Ephraim*, where *Samuel* dwelt, about 20 miles distant from *Jerusalem*, 1 Sam. 1. 1, etc. *Jerom. de loc. Hebraic* calls it *Armatha Sophim, Unde fuit Joseph qui in Evangeliis, ab Arimathi esse scribitur*; whence *Joseph* was; who in the Gospels is called *Joseph* of *Arimathea*. Matthew calls him a rich man, and Mark 15. 43. an honorable counselor. This Evangelist, to counselor, adds the best epithets, *good and just*. All to show how he had the easier access to *Pilate*, and the more ready favor to take down the body of Jesus; which probably a poor obscure man could not have obtained; he was a disciple of Christ, John 19. 38.

**V. 51. ***{waited for the kingdom of God}* He looked for the Redeemer, the *Messias*, and his kingdom.

**V. 52. ***{begged the body, &c.}* Mat. 27. 58. See Mark 15. 43.

**V. 53. ***{never man before was laid}* Thus God's providence disposed it; lest, had he been buried in some old sepulcher, the unbelieving Jews should have said after his resurrection, that it was some other risen from the dead, and not Christ. *Jer. in Mat. 27.* Or that he was raised again by the virtue of some other there interred, as 2 Kings 13. 21.

**V. 54. ***{that day was the preparation, &c.}* Wherein they prepared all necessaries for the feast, which on the Sabbath they might not do, of which it is here said *σάββατον* *ἐπέφωσκε*, that is, did then begin, or drew on; about sunset, for they began the day at even, the word *ἐπιφώσκω* properly signifies *illucesco*, to dawn or wax light, as at break of the day, Mat. 28. 1. but here the word is catachrestically put for *incipiebat*, begun; for their Sabbath began with darkness.

**V. 55. ***{and beheld the sepulchre}* Mat. 27. 61.

**V. 56. ***{according to the commandment}* Which forbade them to do any servile work on the Sabbath days.

## CHAPTER 24

**Verse 1. ***{Upon the first day of the week}* Mat. 28. 1. Mark 16. 1. John 20. 1. Which was the first day after the first Sabbath of the feast, which is the Lord's day; 1 Cor. 16. 2. Rev. 1. 10.

*{very early in the morning}* Mat. 28. 1. Gr. *the deep of the morning*, Mark 16. 2. says *λίαν* *πρωῒ*, *very early*, while the sun was rising, though not very near the *horizon* (for John 20. 1. it was yet dark) but rising toward it, as in the dawning and daybreak; probably it was dark when they began to set out, and much more light before they came to the sepulcher.

*{they came to the sepulchre}* That is, the women which came with Christ from *Galilee*, chap. 23. 55. *Mary Magdalen, Joanna*, and the other *Mary* (verse 10. and chap. 8. 2. Mat. 28.) that is, *Mary* the mother of *James* and *Joses*, also the mother of *Zebedees* children, with many other women, Mat. 27. 55, 56.

**V. 2. ***{stone rolled away}* See Mat. 27. 60, 66. concerning which it seems Mark 16. 3. they were very solicitous, for it was very great and so the rolling it away was beyond their strength.

**V. 3. ***{entered in}* Into the sepulcher, which was hewed out of a rock, like some vault or arched room, or they entered within the enclosure of the sepulcher, and stooping down looked into it. John 20. 5.

**V. 4. ***{two men stood}* That is, two angels, in the form and habit of men. See Acts 1. 19. Gen. 18. 2. Gen. 19. 5.

*{in shining garments}* Of an extraordinary and heavenly luster. The word *ἀστράπτω*, signifies to lighten. *Vatablus* interprets here by *instar fulguris relucentibus. Beza: Vestibus fulgurantibus*; shining like lightning.

**V. 5. ***{bowed down their faces}* As we use in astonishment and fear.

*{they said}* The angels said this unto them.

*{the living}* Him, (that is Christ) who lives.

**V. 6. ***{he is not here}* The humanity of Christ is definite, and his body circumscribed, and therefore cannot be in more places at once.

*{remember how he spake unto you}* Christ had foretold them of his death and resurrection, Mat. 17. 22. Mark 9. 31. John 9. 22. And these women were among the disciples, but understood not, or forgot what they had heard concerning these things; now the angels mind them hereof, that considering that nothing had befallen Christ but that which himself had foretold them, they might believe that he was risen from the dead.

**V. 7. ***{Son of man must be delivered, &c.}* Mat. 17. 22.

*{and be crucified}* Mat. 17. 23.

**V. 8. ***{they remembered his words}* That is, these words which Christ spoke to them in *Galilee*. Mat. 17. 22.

**V. 9. ***{and told all these things}* Mat. 28. 8.

**V. 10. ***{Joanna and Mary}* Chap. 8. 3. *Μαρία* *Ἰακώβου*, mother, or wife of *James*, as *Drusius* thinks. See on Mat. 10. 3.

**V. 11. ***{seemed to them as idle talk}* Gr. *ἐνώπιον* *αὐτῶν*, *coram, in conspectu eorum*, as Luke 1. 6. So the Syriac *בעינוהון* *in oculis eorum*—*before them*: it is a Hebrew phrase importing, *to them*, or their understanding: that is to the apostles, and others which heard these things *ὡσεὶ* *λῆρος*, *nugæ, ineptiæ*, *vain rumors*, or *women's tales*: *Theophylact* interprets *ληρεῖν*, to dote; Syr. *שניותא*, *shoniotho, deliramenta*: of the Hebr. *שנה* *shana*, the Syr. *שנא*, *mutatus fuit mente, insanivit*, See 1 Sam. 21. 13. Prov. 24. 21.) so John 10. 20. for which the Gr. have *μαίνεται*—*he is mad*: the Syr. reads *ומשנא* *שנא* *umeshno sheno, & insaniendo insanit*, he is altogether mad. Thus seemed the women's relation to the apostles, *velut ægri insomnia*, as feverish dreams: supposing them either deluded by some apparition, or some fancies begotten of their own fears: note here, how slowly the seeds of faith take in the thoughts of flesh and blood: we easily believe fables, but hardly truth, however taught or testified.

**V. 12. ***{then arose Peter}* John 20. 6. Though Peter had, out of much infirmity denied Christ, yet he returned to his old love and zeal to him again: such is their condition who sin not of malice, but infirmity: John ran with Peter to the sepulcher, John 20. 3, 4. where they diligently examine all circumstances, they ran with fervent desire of satisfying themselves concerning the report, but with unequal strength: John outran Peter, but Peter first entered the sepulcher.

**V. 13. ***{and behold two of them went}* Mark 16. 12. Two disciples, one is mentioned verse 9. not among the eleven, but as comprehended under those words of the same text, *τοῖς* *λοιποῖς*—to the rest, the disciples (for the eleven, are before expressly mentioned) and named Cleopas, verse 18. whose wife stood by the cross, John 19. 25. Who the other was, is uncertain. *Theoph.* says some say it was Luke. *Epiphanius* *l. 1. hæres. 23. 6.* says, Christ appeared to *Nathanael* and *Cleopa*. It is so uncertain who it was, because he is not named.

*{called Emmaus}* *Jerom. de loc. Hebr.* says it was the place where *Cleopa* dwelt: it was a little old town which the Romans afterward called *Nicopolis*. See *Jerom. Tom. 1. Epitaph. Paul.* The Evangelist seems not to recite it for any excellency of the place, but for the certainty of the history.

*{about threescore furlongs}* About seven miles and a half.

**V. 14. ***{talked together}* As it commonly comes to pass when some extraordinary accident amuses men.

*{which had happened}* Concerning Christ, his death and resurrection reported by the women, and possibly many other things concerning the same, which had so filled their hearts with sorrow, and possessed their minds with a fluctuant desire of knowing what these things would at last come to, that they could hardly intend to think or speak of anything else: probably they lamented the Jews' unworthy usage of him whom they loved and honored, as principally their loss of a Master so incomparably excellent.

**V. 15. ***{while they communed}* Filled with grief they ease their minds, by talking of their yet supposed dead Master: nor could the barbarous ingratitude of the Jews towards Christ, and his sufferings; neither the admirable works which they had seen him do, easily fall out of their minds: now therefore he evidently fulfills that promise, Mat. 18. 20. then in a bodily presence, which he now does by a spiritual; like a traveler by the way he joins with them in company.

**V. 16. ***{but their eyes were holden}* So hindered by the power and providence of God, that they could not know him. See John 20. 14. and 21. 4. in which sense the Syriac says, their eyes were shut: so *Mary Magdalen* saw him in the garden, but knew him not. So were these men's eyes held, not, that they saw no form, but that they mistook the true. For though Christ had now a glorified body, freed from all human necessities; yet was and is it a true body; which he really showed to the witnesses thereto appointed: his state of glory gave immortality, it took not away nature.

**V. 17. ***{and are so sad}* *σκυθρωποὶ*, sour countenanced, as Mat. 6. 16. So men use to look in sorrow.

**V. 18. ***{named Cleopas}* See on verse 13. This seems to be none of the apostles: for verse 33. these men came the same hour to *Jerusalem* and *found the eleven gathered together*, and them that were with them: neither could the other here mentioned, be *Luke*, as may appear, if we consider the preface of the Acts of the Apostles.

*{art thou only a stranger}* The things they talked of were so public, that it seemed strange to them, that any man near Jerusalem could be ignorant thereof.

**V. 19. ***{mighty in word and deed}* Powerful in divine doctrine and miracles. See Acts 7. 22.

*{before God}* Approved for such of God, and the consciences of all good men. See Luke 2. 52.

**V. 20. ***{chief priests and rulers}* For they did that evil, who set on others to do it; the people cried *crucifie him*, but *per Sacerdotum persuasionem. Bernard. de duob. discip. &c.* by the persuasion of the priests: so true is that of *Gregory Nazianzen orat. 1.* *οὐδὲν* *οὕτω* *ῥᾴδιον*, *ὡς* *τὸ* *γενέσθαι* *κακόν*: there is nothing so easy as to be made wicked, though nobody lead or teach us.

**V. 21. ***{but we trusted}* We hoped: they yet understood not what was the deliverance which Christ purchased for the elect, but looked for some worldly kingdom, and therein deliverance from the power of the *Romans*.

**V. 22. ***{women of our company}* Of Christ's disciples. See verse 1.

*{made us astonished}* This sets out their slowness to believe: so far were they from any compact in the matter of the Gospel which they preached to the world; that after many instructions by Christ concerning his rising again the third day, and the event itself demonstrating the truth thereof, themselves were very hardly brought to believe it.

**V. 23. ***{found not his body, &c.}* See verse 3, 4.

**V. 24. ***{certain of them which}* *Peter* and *John*, this was sufficient confirmation of the women's testimony, so that their unbelief was strange, considering that they had the word of Christ to forewarn them, the women's, the two disciples', and the angels' testimony to assure them of the same: no wonder that Christ reproved their incredulity and hardness of heart, Mark 16. 14.

**V. 25. ***{O fools and slow of heart}* *ὦ* *ἀνόητοι*: Syriac, *O mindless men*: it is indeed the greatest foolishness, rather to believe our own reason than God's oracles. Christ hitherto spoke to them in the person of a stranger; that seeming to speak in another man's cause, he might the better persuade them; and that they being not only *Israelites*, but also Christ's disciples, might be ashamed of their unbelief; in that a seeming stranger not only believed, but was able to convince their unbelief, by evident testimonies of the prophets.

**V. 26. ***{ought not Christ}* See 1 Pet. 1. 11. Christ, knowing their thoughts, cures their unbelief, by that which they took for a ground thereof: they thought he could not rise again, neither be the *Messias* promised by the prophets, who suffered an ignominious death, by their hands whom (if he were the *Messias*) he came to save. Christ will therefore show them out of the prophets, that Christ the true *Messias*, promised by the prophets, ought to suffer those very things, which also were foretold by the prophets: and that they ought not to look so much on the Jews' malice and injustice, as on God's decree, who had so appointed it for man's redemption, and foretold it for our confirmation. This (*ought not*) relates to the predictions concerning it: as if he had said; Did not the prophets foretell it? Must not their words be true? See on Matth. 26. 54.

*{and to enter into his glory}* This he says to comfort their drooping minds, who said, verse 21. *we trusted that it had been he*.

**V. 27. ***{and beginning at Moses}* He began to interpret that which was written of Christ by *Moses*; and that which the law prefigured concerning him; as also the writings of the succeeding prophets. See verse 44.

**V. 28. ***{nigh unto the village}* That is, unto *Emaus*.

*{he made as though}* He seemed to them prepared and resolved to go: Gr. *προσεποιεῖτο*; *præ se ferebat*; either going on forward, or by some gesture of taking leave of them: this did Jesus, not to deceive, but to give them an occasion of inviting him to stay with them, that he might opportunely manifest himself unto them.

**V. 29. ***{they constrained him}* They over-entreated him by their importunity, they compelled him by entreaty, not by violence; for they urged that it was near night: probably, as *Jerom* says, *Cleophas* entertained him at his own house, whom he found so expert in the prophets; his heart burning in him with musing who this should be, who so admirably discoursed of these things.

**V. 30. ***{and blessed it}* According to the custom of the Jews, among whom the master of the family, or chief man of the company, gave thanks, or prayed; and then distributed the bread, or carved to all the company. See Matth. 14. 19. It is probable that Christ used some accustomed form of thankfulness, or else some other action or gesture, by which these men knew him again, verse 35.

**V. 31. ***{vanished out of their sight}* Or *ceased to be seen of them*; by a miraculous and unusual manner they lost sight of him.

**V. 32. ***{did not our hearts burn}* Were we not inwardly moved, as by a divine fervor of God's Spirit? It intimates a sovereign power and authority in the words of Christ. See Mat. 7. 29. John 7. 47. Psal. 45. 2. Psal. 39. 4. This shows that they were terrified and thoughtful, as verse 37, 38. in which the sudden recourse of the vital spirits to their fountain the heart, use (as it were by thronging it) to make it hot and throbbing with extraordinary pulses.

**V. 33. ***{and they rose up the same hour}* Considering the sorrow into which the disciples were now plunged, they thought long to comfort them, by assuring them that Christ was risen from the dead: therefore leaving their own present affairs and rest, they intend that which was far more necessary: such was their joy, and desire to communicate the same to others: all private interest must give place to God's honor, and the comfort and salvation of souls. Nor may we therein defer that until tomorrow which can be done today.

**V. 34. ***{saying, the Lord is risen indeed}* It must needs be late at night, when these two came back after supper from *Emaus* to *Jerusalem* to the disciples; yet they found them together conferring about the passion and resurrection of Christ: and they who came to bring comfort to them, found it of them: thus God rewarded their mutual care to confirm each other: they tell how Christ was risen and appeared to *Cephas*: see 1 Cor. 15. 4. that which we read, Mark 16. 13. *neither believed they them*, is to be understood distributively; as if he said, some (as *Thomas*, John 20. 25.) believed not yet: but some of them believed.

**V. 35. ***{what things were done in the way}* Though yet they were not fully settled, yet they conferred testimonies; so confirming each other: faith has its infancy and growth.

**V. 36. ***{as they thus spake}* Mark 16. 14. John 20. 19.

*{peace be unto you}* It was a usual manner of salutation with them: as we use to say, *God save you*, as chap. 10. 5.

**V. 37. ***{but they were terrified}* Though they now confidently believed his resurrection, yet the suddenness of his appearing startled them: possibly they might think it rather presented unto them in a vision; than that Jesus himself, lately crucified, was now standing alive by them: so hard is it to believe divine and certain truths; so timorous is unbelief (See Levit. 26. 36.) Faith gives courage or confidence in greatest dangers (Psalm 23. 4. Psalm 46. 3.) but unbelief causes fear, where no fear is. See Mat. 14. 25.

**V. 38. ***{why do thoughts arise?}* That is, perplexities, and doubts concerning some delusion.

**V. 39. ***{handle me, &c.}* See John 20. 20. He admits the testimony of their own senses to assure them it was no vision, or spirit; but a true and real body risen from the dead, which they now saw: whatever is essential to a true body glorified, is yet in Christ's body: *Fulgentius de fide orthod. ad Donat.* says well; if any shall so preach the truth of the Deity in Christ, *ut ejus veram carnem negare contendat, non est Christianus catholicus, sed Hæreticus*, *&c.* as that he should strive to deny the truth of his flesh, or that his flesh is true flesh, he is not a catholic Christian, but a heretical Manichee: and *Augustine ep. 146. Consentio; ego proinde Domini Corpus ita in cœlo esse credo, ut erat in terra quando ascendit in cœlum, &c.* I therefore believe, that the body of our Lord is in heaven, so as it was on earth, when he ascended into heaven: for he said to his disciples, *See my hands and my feet, handle me and see, &c.* and again a little after he says, therefore that flesh was then a spiritual body, yet was it not a spirit, but a body: See 1 Cor. 15. 44. if a true body obvious to sense, then circumscribed (it could not else be seen and felt) and so not carnally in the sacrament under the species of bread and wine.

**V. 40. ***{he shewed them his hands and his feet}* With the prints of the nails wherewith he was fastened to the cross. See John 20. 27. He that with a touch healed *Malchus'* ear, could as easily have healed and taken away both wounds and scars; but he who rose again for us, would for our sakes yet bear these remains of the cross for a time; that he might assure us, that it was he, who was indeed the first fruits of the dead: we may not think he retains these still; if while yet he bore mortal flesh and blood, his face shined like the sun in his brightness, and his clothes were white as the light; what is he now in his divine glory? as he would eat before his disciples (who had no need of meat) to testify his resurrection, so would he yet bear these scars: by all this, the Spirit working thereon, he fitted them with great power to testify unto the world the resurrection of the Lord Jesus, Acts 4. 33.

**V. 41. ***{have ye any meat?}* See John 21. 5. To which place of the history, these words here recorded by St. *Luke* (who for brevity's sake omitted many things, which happened between this appearing of Christ, and that at the sea of *Tiberias*, Joh. 21. 1, etc.) appertain.

**V. 42. ***{a piece of a broiled fish}* John 21. 9, 13.

**V. 43. ***{and did eat before them}* Not that he was now subject to human necessities, but only for the greater confirmation of his disciples' faith.

**V. 44. ***{these are the words}* As if he had said, this is that which I told you concerning my death, burial, resurrection, etc. and whatever else was foretold of me: therefore this should not seem any strange or new thing unto you.

**V. 45. ***{Then opened he their understanding}* As Acts 16. 14. by his Holy Spirit giving them understanding: the word of God shines as a true light, yet as the cloudy pillar was darkness to the Egyptians; so is this to carnal and corrupted minds: it is spiritually discerned, 1 Cor. 2. until the Holy Ghost open the understanding, it cannot apprehend: so much need is there of the Apostle's rule, *Let him become a fool, that he may be wise*, 1 Cor. 3. 18. there is no worse cloud to obscure the light of the Spirit, than confidence in our own wisdom. See 2 Cor. 4. 3, 4. Psal. 119. 18. 1 Cor. 3. 7.

**V. 46. ***{thus it is written}* Isa. 53. 7. Ps. 22. 16. Ps. 16. 10. Jonah 2. 10. Hos. 6. 2. It seems he cited these, or some of these, and the like places of Scripture, concerning Christ's sufferings; as for example—*he is brought as a lambe to the slaughter, &c.* *they pierced my hands and my feet, &c.* by which he evidently proved, that in all his passion nothing befell him, but that which the Spirit of God had foretold by the prophets should come to pass: which much takes off from the scandal of the cross, when it appears that all came to pass by the determinate counsel of God; and serves to the confirmation and certain evidence of the truth of Christ, seeing that all that the law prefigured in the *Messias*, and whatever any prophecies foretold concerning him, were truly and really fulfilled in *Jesus Christ*, the son of the *Virgin Mary*.

*{thus it behoved}* That is, *necessitate consequentiæ*, because God's counsel had so appointed to accomplish man's redemption, by the death and resurrection of Christ.

**V. 47. ***{should be preached in his name}* By his commission and authority; as concerning preaching of the word: and by his grace and Holy Spirit, as concerning the effect thereof in remission of sins. He began to preach repentance and remission of sins, Mat. 4. 17. and after his resurrection commissioned his apostles and successors in the ministry to do the same. See 2 Cor. 5. 20. Acts 2. 38. Acts 10. 42, 43. Or *his name, &c.* that is, by his merit and mediation remission of sins is to all that believe in him.

*{among all nations}* Psalm 22. 27. Psalm 2. 8. Isaiah 49. 6. Michah 4. 2. Isaiah 60. 1. See chap. 2. 32. So he gave them commission to do, Mat. 28. 19. Mark 16. 15.

**V. 48. ***{ye are witnesses}* You have been witnesses of that which hitherto passed: and you shall be my witnesses hereof unto all nations (Acts 1. 8.) concerning my doctrine, miracles, death, resurrection, and ascension into heaven.

**V. 49. ***{the promise of my Father}* He says *ἀποστέλλω*, I do send, for, I will send, as the Syriac gives it; because he would shortly send: he calls it the promise, of his Father, because God promised it by him: the promise, that is, the gifts and graces of the Holy Ghost promised them. See John 14. 6. and 15. 26. Isa. 44. 3. Ezek. 36. 26. Joel 2. 28.

*{but tarry ye}* You who are now fearful, stay at Jerusalem expecting the fulfilling of this promise to confirm and assure you: thus will he have them stay, till they are completely armed.

*{endued with power}* Until you be furnished with the gifts of tongues and healing; as also with enlarged hearts, enabling you to perform the great work to which I have assigned you: and also the spirit of divine and undoubted fortitude.

**V. 50. ***{and he led them out as far as, &c.}* Understand this leading out to be on the fortieth day after his resurrection; for so long he was seen of them. Bethany was near Jerusalem, on the mount of Olives, Mark 11. 1. Acts 1. 12. Christ being to ascend into heaven, leads his disciples hither, out of Jerusalem, lest being seen, there might arise new troubles: and because he reserved the knowledge thereof to certain witnesses thereto foreordained.

*{and blessed them}* Like a father before his departure, praying for his children, and blessing them. See Gen. 27. 4. and 48. 9. and 49. 1, 2. That he lifted up his hand, was a fit gesture for prayer. See Exodus 17. 11. and 19. 11. as it were expressing whence they expected the blessing, 1 Tim. 2. 8. which seems a very dictate of nature, teaching men that heaven is the throne of God; *καὶ* *ὑπέρτατα* *δώματα* *ναίει*, and that he dwells in the highest habitations; so also another, *duplices tendens ad sydera palmas*.

**V. 51. ***{and it came to pass}* Mark 16. 19. Acts 1. 9. We may not here, with some, understand that this came to pass suddenly, before he had finished the blessing: this *ἐν* *τῷ* *εὐλογεῖν* is used also for *postquam, &c.* after that; as Luke 14. 1. *ἐν* *τῷ* *ἐλθεῖν* is put for *μετὰ* *τὸ* *ἐλθεῖν*—after he came; so Mark 16. 19. interprets this, *ἐν*, by *μετὰ* *τὸ* *λαλῆσαι* *αὐτοῖς*—*after the Lord had spoken unto them*: the Syriac here gives, *ἐν* *τῷ* *εὐλογεῖν*, by *דכד* *cum benediceret*, that is, *postquam benedixerat*; for such is the value of that particle with an infinitive, *importat significationem temporis præteriti. Schindl. Pent.*

**V. 52. ***{and they worshipped him}* They fixed their eyes upon him, as far as they could see him, until the cloud took him out of their sight, and the angels admonished them to look no longer after him with bodily eyes, Acts 1. 9, etc. which when they understood, they adored him in spirit, and confession of him their Lord and Savior, to whom all power in heaven and earth is given.

**V. 53. ***{were continually}* That is daily: for otherwise we cannot understand that, Acts 1. 13, 14. concerning their abode in an upper room, then, that they daily met there, and had constant and frequent assemblies there: so here we must understand this, *διαπαντὸς*, *continually*, that is frequently, or daily.

*{Amen}* This word imports the consummation and certainty of all that which the Evangelist has written. See on Mat. 28. 20. Mark 16. 20. and on John 3. 11.

**END OF THE ANNOTATIONS ON THE GOSPEL ACCORDING TO S. LUKE. **
